AMENDMENT TO CONTRACT BETWEEN  
THE CITY OF CHEYENNE AND CHARLES E. BALES 
 
This Amendment to City Contract #3920 is made and entered into by and between the City 
of Cheyenne, a municipality organized and existing under the laws of the State of Wyoming, whose 
address is 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming 82001 (“City”), and Charles E. Bales, whose 
address is 3618 Charles Street, Cheyenne, Wyoming 82009 (“Mr. Bales”). 
 
 
RECITALS 
 
WHEREFORE Mr. Bales is the current owner of property (“the property”) which is 
contiguous to, but not annexed to, the City.  The property is more specifically described as 
follows: 
 
Lots 6 and 7, Robitaille Subdivision, and all of that part of Robitaille Court and 
Wills Road adjacent to Lots 6 and 7, and that portion of Charles Street from the 
west right-of-way line of Wills Road easterly 135 feet, Laramie County, Wyoming. 
 
WHEREFORE, on March 21, 2000, pursuant to City Contract # 3920, Mr. Bales applied 
to annex the property to the City in order to obtain City sewer service because his private sewer 
system had failed. Mr. Bales was not able to finance the construction of street improvements 
including street, sidewalk, curb and gutter, which were required by the City as a condition 
precedent to annexation and availability of City sewer service. 
 
WHEREFORE, the City recognized that it was important to annex Mr. Bales’ property 
because such action would resolve a serious health and safety problem, diminish county pockets 
and add residents to the tax rolls of the City. 
 
WHEREFORE, as a result, the City and Mr. Bales entered into City Contract # 3920 
wherein he acknowledged that he is obligated to pay all costs associated with the construction of 
street improvements including street, sidewalk, curb and gutter, upon the City’s request.  He also 
acknowledged that the improvements will be made when his property is sold or subject to a 
Development Action. 
 
WHEREFORE, the definition of a “Development Action” means an action improving a 
site with a structural improvement requiring a building or other permit and intended to be a 
permanent improvement of the Development as its operation is contemplated herein (i.e., vertical 
data center buildings or permanent road or utility installation, but not including permanent 
improvements by a public or private utility provider, and not including site grading of the 
Property). “Development Action” is used in the UDC, but not defined in City Contract #3920. The 
Parties agree to use the foregoing definition for purposes of interpretation of any application of 
such term under the UDC for purposes of all matters related to this Amended Contract. 
 
WHEREFORE, since that time, because Mr. Bales’ property is surrounded on three sides 
by a gravel street, Mr. Bales has discovered that the costs associated with the construction of street 
4:07 pm, Jan 12 2024

Page 2 of 4 
improvements including street, sidewalk, curb and gutter were financially untenable. 
 
WHEREFORE, the City Engineer has reviewed the site, contract, and projected 
constructions for all adjacent roadway infastructure would result in undue financial burden to Mr. 
Bales or his successors in interest.  
 
WHEREFORE, Mr. Bales is now requesting that City Contract # 3920 be amended to 
permit him to only make the required improvements when his property is sold. 
 
THEREFORE, in consideration of the mutual promises and covenants contained in this 
agreement, the parties agree as follows: 
 
1. 
The City hereby agrees to defer all offsite roadway construction of the above-
described improvements until such time that a multifamily residential or commercial development 
action is constructed on the property. 
 
2. 
Furthermore, the City agrees that Mr. Bales or his successors in interest shall be 
deemed to have meet all the obligations of this or the previous agreement by provide a cash in lieu 
amount for one half of the Public Improvements on Charles Street adjacent to Lot 6 and 7 of the 
Robitaille Subdivision, Laramie County, Wyoming as outline in Exhibit A.  The term “Cash-In-
Lieu” shall mean those payments made by Developer in advance pursuant to UDC Sections 
4.1.5.b.6 and 4.1.5.b.7 to satisfy in full Developer’s obligations with respect to certain specified 
Public Improvements as set forth herein. 
 
3. 
Both parties agree that the Cash in Lieu Amounts may be utilized by the City for 
construction or maintenance of any roadway public improvement within the City of Cheyenne.  
However, this provision does not relieve the City’s responsibility to either complete the Public 
Improvements on Charles Street or reimburse a future developer for the lesser amount of either 
the accepted improvements cost, or the costs outlined in Exhibit A.  
 
4. 
Reimbursement Schedule.  Mr. Bales or his successors in interest agree to provide 
a Cash-in-Lieu amount to the City of Cheyenne as outlined in paragraph K within thirty (30) days 
of the sale, which includes entering into a contract for deed or similar arrangement.  Both parties 
acknowledge that further development action on the subject properties shall be subject to required 
offsite and onsite improvements in accordance with the current codes and ordinances at the time 
of development. 
 
III. 
GENERAL PROVISIONS. 
 
A. 
Applicable Law.  The construction, interpretation and enforcement of this 
Agreement shall be governed by the laws of the State of Wyoming.  The courts of the State of 
Wyoming shall have jurisdiction over any action arising out of this Agreement and over the parties, 
and the venue shall be the First Judicial District, Laramie County, Wyoming. 
 
B. 
Entirety of Agreement.  This Agreement, consisting of four (4) pages, represents 

Page 3 of 4 
the entire and integrated agreement between the parties and supersedes all prior negotiations, 
representations and agreements, whether written or oral.  None of terms of this Agreement may 
be waived or modified to any extent. 
 
C. 
Governmental Immunity.  The City of Cheyenne does not waive its 
governmental immunity by entering into this Agreement, and fully retains all immunities and 
defenses provided by law with respect to any action based on or occurring as a result of this 
Agreement.  Mr. Bales further agrees to indemnify and hold harmless the City, its officers, agents, 
employees and representatives from any claim or liability arising out of the failure to comply with 
the terms and conditions of this Agreement. 
 
D. 
Binding Nature of Agreement.  The terms and conditions contained in this 
Agreement shall be a covenant running with the land and shall extend to and be binding upon Mr. 
Bales, his heirs, devisees, personal representatives, successors, assigns and any subsequent 
purchaser, lessee or holder of a contract for deed on the property. 
 
E. 
Encumbrance.  This Amended Contract shall be put of record by the Laramie 
County Clerk of Deeds and shall operate as an encumbrance on the property.  The amount of the 
encumbrance shall be actual costs of construction as determined by the City Engineer or one 
hundred and fifty percent (150%) of the minimum estimated cost of the improvements on that date 
if actual costs are not determined by the time of sale. 
 
IN WITNESS WHEREOF, the parties to this Agreement have executed this Agreement 
on the dates set out below, and certify that they have read, understood and agreed to the terms and 
conditions of this Agreement as set forth herein. 
 
The effective date of this Agreement is the date of the signature last affixed to this page. 
 
Dated this ______ day of _____________________, 2024. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
City of Cheyenne

Contract No.___ 
 
CONTRACT MODIFICATION BETWEEN  
THE CITY OF CHEYENNE AND CHARLES E. BALES 
 
This Amendment to City Contract #3920 is made and entered into by and between the City 
of Cheyenne, a municipality organized and existing under the laws of the State of Wyoming, whose 
address is 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming 82001 (“City”), and Charles E. Bales, whose 
address is 3618 Charles Street, Cheyenne, Wyoming 82009 (“Bales”). 
 
 
RECITALS 
 
WHEREAS, the City and owner entered into a City Contract #3920 (Contract) on May 8, 
2000, regarding deferral of improvements required at time of annexation for the purposes of 
connecting to the City sewer system; 
 
WHEREAS, the Contract was based upon City municipal code requirements at the time of 
annexation; 
 
WHEREAS, on August 22, 2022, the governing body approved Ordinance No. 4456 
amending Chapter 1.16 of municipal code to not require infrastructure improvements at time of 
annexation or connection to City water or sewer service; 
 
WHEREAS, the city does require infrastructure improvements at time of development 
actions as described by the current code; 
 
WHEREAS, the improvements in the Contract would not be required for an identical 
situation if initiated under present ordinance; and 
 
WHEREAS, the City has annexed the property in accordance with Section I, City’s 
Obligations, of the Contract. 
 
THEREFORE, in consideration of the mutual promises and covenants contained in this 
agreement, the parties agree to modification of the Contract as follows: 
 
1. 
That Section 1, City’s Obligations is modified to no longer require the promise that 
Bales will perform the improvements within thirty (30) days of notice by the City Engineer or sale 
of the property; 
 
2. 
That the City considers the obligations of the owner or his successors in interest at 
time of annexation met. The improvements as described in the recitals and Section II, Bales’ 
Obligations, of the Contract shall no longer be required in association with annexation; and 
 
3. 
That public improvements shall be installed in accordance with public 
improvement requirements at the time a development action occurs on the subject lands in 
11:07 am, Feb 20 2024

Page 2 of 2 
accordance with current development ordinances in effect at the time of such development action. 
 
IN WITNESS WHEREOF, the parties to this Agreement have executed this Agreement 
on the dates set out below, and certify that they have read, understood, and agreed to the terms and 
conditions of this Agreement as set forth herein. 
 
The effective date of this Agreement is the date of the signature last affixed to this page. 
 
Dated this ______ day of _____________________, 2024. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
City of Cheyenne

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2024-02-06_TotalExtraction_78978994.pdf
CITY OF CHEYENNE 
CONTRACT MODIFICATION 
 
Contract Modification:  #3 
Project:  Happy Jack Landfill Environmental Monitoring Program 
Project Number:  Contract # 6798 
Contractor:  Trihydro Corporation 
Date Prepared:  January 29, 2024 
 
The above referenced contract is amended or modified as set forth in the following and these 
amendments and modifications of the plans and specifications are agreed to by the parties. All other 
aspects of the original agreement remain unchanged and in full force and effect. 
 
I. Description of Changes 
 
The changes provide for an additional renewal of the Contract #6798, Happy Jack Environmental 
Monitoring. The Contract will be extended for one (1) additional year, from March 1, 2024 
through March 1, 2025. Trihydro agrees to perform all onsite well monitoring and testing as 
detailed in the original contract. 
 
This modification will increase the amount of the contract by $94,242. 
 
II. New Contract Items 
 
All items to remain the same as the original contract. 
 
III. Schedule 
 
Groundwater Sampling and Analysis shall be performed in the field during the months of April 
2024 and August 2024. 
 
Methane Monitoring shall be performed in the Office and Scale House, Landfill Equipment 
Shelter Shop Area, the fourteen (14) methane monitoring wells surrounding the permitted 
boundary, and the five (5) newly installed passive gas vents along the west perimeter road. 
Methane monitoring shall be performed in the field during the months of March 2024, June 
2024, September 2024, and December 2024 
 
IV. Summary

City of Cheyenne  
 
 
 
 
DATE:____________________________ 
 
 
_______________________________________/______________________________________ 
Mayor Patrick Collins  
 
 
      /ATTEST: City Clerk Kristina F. Jones 
 

 
 
 
 
 
January 19, 2024 
 
 
 
Mr. Samuele Quattrini 
Landfill Manager 
City of Cheyenne 
1461 Happy Jack Rd. 
Cheyenne, WY  82009 
 
RE: 
2024 Proposal for Conducting Routine Environmental Monitoring Activities for the City of 
Cheyenne Sanitary Landfill (Happy Jack Landfill), SHWD File #10.080, Cheyenne, Wyoming 
 
Dear Mr. Quattrini: 
 
Trihydro Corporation appreciates the opportunity to provide this cost proposal to the City of Cheyenne 
(City) for the 2024 environmental monitoring at the Cheyenne Sanitary Landfill (Landfill).  This proposal 
is for a one-year extension of the existing Happy Jack Landfill Environmental Monitoring Contract, 
#6798, between the City of Cheyenne and Trihydro Corporation, set to expire on March 1, 2024.  Work 
will fulfill requirements permitted under the Sanitary Landfill Environmental Monitoring Plan (SLEMP) 
originally dated April 12, 2017, and revised March 14, 2019. 
 
SCOPE OF WORK 
Our project understanding and the scope of work for each proposed task is outlined below for your review 
and consideration.  The scope of work was developed based on our experience conducting the 
environmental monitoring at the Landfill since 2002 and from our experience working with the Wyoming 
Department of Environmental Quality, Solid & Hazardous Waste Division (WDEQ/SHWD) on dozens of 
sites across Wyoming.  A detailed cost estimate for each task and a description of related activities and 
assumptions is provided as Attachment A.   
 
Task 1A and 2A – April and August Environmental Monitoring 
Semiannual groundwater monitoring will be conducted in April and August whereby static water levels 
will be gauged in up to 40 wells and 2 leachate sumps, and groundwater samples collected from up to 
36 wells and 1 leachate sump.  Samples will be submitted for laboratory analysis of the Wyoming Solid 
Waste Rules and Regulations (WSWRR), Chapter 2, Appendix A, B, and/or C constituents, as designated 
by the SLEMP.  Methane monitoring of structures and perimeter wells will also be conducted during 
these semiannual events.   
 
Our 2024 cost estimate for the April and August semiannual monitoring events is $59,283, which 
includes an estimated $28,875 for laboratory services.  Despite our aggressive and beneficial negotiations 

 
 
 
 
 
Mr. Sam Quattrini 
January 19, 2024 
Page 2 
 
 
 
with three competitive laboratories over the last year, analytical costs are almost double what they were 
six years ago; costs ballooned in the wake of COVID during a period of large laboratory mergers, supply 
issues, inflation, and courier costs.  Based on our most recent experience and negotiations, Trihydro 
believes it most advantageous to remain with Eurofins Denver (formerly TestAmerica Denver) as they are 
currently providing reliable and economical service.  A copy of the related quote and annual extension is 
provided as Attachment B.  The cost at similarly capable laboratories would be, at a minimum, 
20% higher, which is equivalent to $6,000 or more. 
 
Based on our relationship with Eurofins paired with thoughtful and effective negotiations, Eurofins has 
agreed to honor last year’s quote through 2024, including a flat rate for standard overnight shipping at 
$60/cooler.  Many laboratories are no longer providing pre-paid shipping due to the erratic market.  If 
Eurofins had not extended their shipping agreement, shipping costs would have increased by at least 
$190/cooler, or $4,750 per year (assuming 25 coolers).  We are pleased that our negotiations are resulting 
in a substantial cost savings to the City.    
 
Task 1B and 2B – April and August Environmental Reporting 
Semiannual reports will be prepared for the April and August environmental monitoring events and 
include data management, data validation, and report preparation.  Reports will provide a narrative of 
field, laboratory, validation activities, and results as well as observations, conclusions, and 
recommendations.  The report will also provide relevant tables, figures, and appendices, as outlined in 
Attachment A.  Our 2024 cost estimate for the April and August semiannual reporting is $14,320. 
 
Task 3 – Two Quarterly Methane Monitoring Events 
Under Task 3, two quarterly methane monitoring events will be conducted in February and December.  
During each event, 14 perimeter methane probes, five passive gas vents, and four structures (scale house, 
office, shop area, and equipment building) will be visited to measure and verify that methane 
concentrations are within permissible limits.  (The other two quarterly events will be covered under Tasks 
1A and 1B, in April and August.)  Our 2024 cost estimate for this task is $4,006.   
 
Task 4 – Annual Environmental Reporting  
Following collection of all data required for monitoring during 2024, an Annual Report will be prepared 
to summarize findings over the course of the year.  Statistical analysis of groundwater in each of the 
three water-bearing zones will also be conducted under this task.  Our proposed budget for this task is 
$11,633, including approximately $4,800 for the statistical analysis of the three water-bearing zones.
 

 
 
 
 
 
Mr. Sam Quattrini 
January 19, 2024 
Page 3 
 
 
 
202401_CheyenneLandfillEnvMon_V2_LTRPROP.docx 
Task 5 – Optional Technical Assistance  
For the coming year, we have included a task dedicated to optional technical assistance.  For many of 
our Wyoming landfill projects, Trihydro includes a budget for technical and regulatory assistance for 
unanticipated tasks that may arise during the year.  If agreeable, Trihydro proposes a $5,000 technical and 
regulatory assistance budget in the event work is requested to address issues that are outside of existing 
work orders (e.g., responses to new regulatory issues, additional site activities, well repairs, etc.).  No 
work would be completed under this task without written approval from the City.  For any activities 
exceeding the $5,000 allocated for this task, Trihydro will prepare a separate cost proposal. 
 
CLOSING 
Our overall cost estimate for the 2024 Environmental Monitoring and Reporting project is $94,242 and 
our Standard Schedule of Charges is included as Attachment C.  As always, Trihydro will strive to 
complete the requested activities as efficiently and cost-effectively as possible, and we are available to 
start the work upon receipt of a purchase order.  If you have any questions or require additional 
information regarding this proposal and/or contracts, please do not hesitate to contact us at 
(307) 745-7474. 
 
Sincerely,  
Trihydro Corporation 
 
 
 
Caroline Brewer, P.G.  
 
 
 
Marjorie Bedessem, Ph.D, P.E. 
Project Manager 
 
 
 
 
Project Director

Eurofins Environment Testing America Quotation Number 28024157
Dear Caroline Brewer:
We appreciate the opportunity to provide your company with a quotation for your Wyoming Landfills 2023 Multi-Sites project. 
Eurofins Environment Testing America has a unique combination of full service capabilities, technical expertise, local service 
options, and online resources necessary to ensure successful project outcomes. 
At Eurofins Environment Testing America, quality is the hallmark of our business.  To ensure your project’s data quality 
objectives are met, we offer experienced personnel who are trained and committed to completing your analytical project on 
time, a fully documented QA/QC program, and state-of-the-art laboratory equipment and facilities. In addition to being a full 
service laboratory, we are part of the nation’s largest environmental laboratory network. This provides access to an 
unparalleled spectrum of capabilities and turnaround time options, all through a single point of contact.  Janice Winn-Shilling 
has been assigned as your Project Manager for this work and can be reached by phone at 303-736-0119 or via email at 
Janice.Winn-Shilling@ET.eurofinsUS.com.
·
MyEOL®,  a web portal offering you customizable, real time access to data. With 24 hour access you can perform data 
trending, compare data to industry or project limits, track CoCs, invoices, reports and much more.
·
Level IV Deliverables/Customizable EDDs:  high resolution, text searchable reports, available in virtually any format.
·
Extensive Experience: Project Managers with in-depth knowledge of regulatory protocols and procedures.
·
Nationwide Logistical Support: bringing you an extensive courier network, service centers and shipping options 
throughout the U.S. and abroad. 
·
PFAS, Dioxins/Furans, Air, Radiochemistry, IH and other specialty analyses are offered alongside routine soil and 
water methods with seamless reports and consolidated EDDs. 
The following quotation includes a detailed price breakdown, as well as any notes and clarifications pertaining to your project, 
and is subject to Eurofins Environment Testing America's Standard Terms and Conditions, unless otherwise agreed upon in 
writing.  
We thank you for choosing Eurofins Environment Testing America, and we look forward to working with you on this project.

PROJECT DETAILS
Clarifications and Exceptions
Footniote 1:
Eurofins Denver laboratory does not report p-Phenylene diamine by 8270E as target 
analyte. This compound will be reported as Tentatively Identified Compound (TIC).
SampleReceiving Hours/Hold 
Time/TAT
Sample Receiving Hours
8 am to 5 pm Monday - Friday
Samples received at the laboratory after 11 AM are considered received on the following 
business day (Monday-Friday, no holidays). We will do our best to meet rush received after 3 
pm, but to better ensure success this should be arranged with your PM prior to sample 
submittal. Eurofins Environment Testing America Denver accepts Saturday deliveries via Fed 
Ex and client drop off every Saturday from 9-12.  You will need to request Saturday delivery 
when you ship or Fed Ex will hold the samples until Monday. Please note that samples 
received on Saturday will not be processed until the next business day.  
Hold Time
Eurofins Environment Testing America, Inc. requests that samples be hand delivered the same 
day of collection or shipped to the laboratory via overnight priority service on the day of 
collection.  For samples received with less than one half the holding time remaining, Eurofins 
Environment Testing America will make our best effort to extract and analyze these samples 
within the holding time; however, Eurofins Environment Testing America will not be held 
responsible for meeting holding times if samples are received with less than one half the 
holding time remaining. For methods that require a short holding time (less than 7 calendar 
days from collection), Eurofins TestAmerica requests prior notification before samples are 
shipped to the laboratory. Samples that have to be analyzed and/or extracted on 
expedited turnaround time to meet hold time will incur a surcharge rate. 
Turnaround Time
·      Quoted Turnaround Time - Data will be delivered at the proposed turnaround time in 
Business Days from Sample Receipt unless otherwise agreed upon. TAT begins the day 
the laboratory performing analyses receives the samples (day of lab receipt = day zero).  
Samples received after 11 AM will be considered received the next business day.
·      Expedited Turnaround Time:  Expedited turnaround times may be available and must be 
pre-approved by the laboratory.  Expedited turnaround delivery is contingent upon meeting 
the agreed upon delivery date/time and number of samples.  Samples received after 11 AM 
will be considered received the next business day.  Results will be provided via e-mail or 
TotalAccess by close of business in the lab's time zone unless another time has been 
agreed to in advance. 
 
Expedited turnaround time surcharges for standard analyses are:
-   5 Business Days TAT = 30%
-   4 Business Days TAT = 45%
-   3 Business Days TAT = 60%
-   2 Business Days TAT = 75%
-   1 Business Day TAT = 100%

Eurofins TestAmerica Business 
T&Cs
Confidentiality -
This quote has been prepared by TestAmerica Laboratories, Inc. dba Eurofins TestAmerica 
(Eurofins TestAmerica), solely for the use of the customer to whom it is addressed in evaluating 
Eurofins TestAmerica's qualifications and capabilities in connection with a particular project. 
The user of this document agrees by its acceptance to return it to Eurofins TestAmerica upon 
request and not to reproduce, copy, lend, or otherwise disclose its contents, directly or 
indirectly, and not to use it for any purpose other than that for which it was specifically provided. 
The user also agrees that where consultants or other outside parties are involved in the 
evaluation process, access to this document shall not be given to said parties unless those 
parties also specifically agree to these conditions. In the absence of signed acceptance, 
submittal of samples will indicate acceptance of this quotation.
Terms and Conditions - 
This quotation is based solely upon Eurofins TestAmerica's standard product (routine QA/QC, 
detection limits, deliverables and standard turnaround times) and noted exceptions herein.  The 
discounts incorporated into the pricing are based upon the sample quantity, test method, and 
schedule quoted.  Any deviations may impact pricing and/or the acceptance of work. Final 
acceptance of this work is contingent upon a mutually agreed Sample Delivery Schedule. All 
sales are subject to Eurofins TestAmerica's Terms and Conditions unless alternative terms 
have been agreed to in writing.  Submittal of samples will indicate acceptance of this 
quotation.
Quote Expiration - 
Pricing listed in the proposal will expire 90 days from the quote date unless the project is 
awarded/confirmed within that time period.  Unless otherwise set forth in this quotation, Eurofins 
TestAmerica reserves the right to re-evaluate pricing for extended length projects on an annual 
basis.
SERWM -  
A fee, notated as Safe and Environmentally Responsible Waste Management (SERWM), will be 
applied to all invoices for each sample processed by the laboratory.
PROJECT SETUP
Field Sampling Products -
Eurofins TestAmerica can provide field sampling products upon request and these products are 
billable to the client at the time of shipment.
 
Field sampling products may include, but are not limited to:
·      VOA sampling devices, preservation kits, ISM supplies, tubing and filters
·      Industrial Hygiene media, surface sampling wipes, source sampling traps
 
The cost of the soil sampling devices is not included in the quoted rates for volatile analysis.  
Core Samplers
·      5 gram Core N’ One and EnCore samplers are available at $10 each (three required per 
sample).  
·      Disposable Core N’ One T-Handles are available at $15 each.
·      Eurofins TestAmerica does not supply EnCore T-Handles.

Coolers and Sampling Supplies - 
·      Sampling Supplies: Eurofins TestAmerica will provide sample containers and coolers to 
support the sampling of water and soil samples.  Extra bottles may be provided (up to 10%)

returned to the lab, including empty coolers and a reasonable percentage of the projected 
sample load - 70% or higher of the expected/quoted sample number.  Coolers not received 
back by the projected deadline or as agreed with the PM may be charged at $30 per 
cooler.  Similarly, if the sample containers received as samples are less than 90% of the 
containers provided, the sample containers not received as samples will be charged at a 
flat rate of $1 per container.
 
·      Sample Container Shipping: The containers and preservatives required by the project shall 
be delivered via ground transportation.  A minimum of 5 business days advance notice is 
required in order to achieve shipment by ground transportation.  Supply shipments 
requiring priority delivery due to insufficient lead time for ground transportation shall be 
charged to the client at Eurofins TestAmerica's cost.  Alternatively, Eurofins TestAmerica 
can ship the supplies via carrier of choice by the client using the client's shipping account.
Eurofins TestAmerica does not supply wet ice or blue ice for shipments.  If shipping 
containers are not returned within 60 days, a charge for the containers will be billed at the 
current market rate. Please contact your PM for the current charges.
Courier Services and Sample Pick-Up - 
Courier Services are offered by some Eurofins TestAmerica facilities.  Where offered, the cost 
of the services will vary based on the distance traveled, the scope of the project being 
supported, and whether sufficient notice (typically 48 hours) is provided to facilitate efficient 
scheduling.  If no details are described in this quotation and you are interested in learning more 
about courier options, please contact your Client Relations Manager or Project Manager to 
inquire about availability and cost.
Minimum Log-In Charges - 
Eurofins TestAmerica’s minimum charge is $150 for a group of samples received together for 
analysis.  Our minimum invoice value is $150.

PROJECT DELIVERABLES
Eurofins TestAmerica will provide two analytical report formats, a final report in PDF format and 
a standard Eurofins TestAmerica EDD.  Both electronic report formats will be delivered via email 
or web portal.  If additional formats or retroactive deliverables are requested, costs of report 
generation will be billable.  Charges will be based on labor and materials cost of report 
generation and data retrieval.  Please contact your PM to inquire about availability and the price 
of additional deliverables.
·      Report Format: Unless a level III or IV deliverable is specifically listed on the pricing page, 
this quotation includes delivery of a Level I or II report.  Level III or IV reports are available 
at an additional charge.
·      Electronic Data Deliverable Format: Eurofins TestAmerica has many EDD formats 
available to our clients including the most widely used commercial formats.  Eurofins 
TestAmerica offers data using a standard EDD.  Other EDD formats are available for a 
minimal cost of $25 per format (if not included as part of the report options listed in the 
quotation).  The development of EDD formats that are not already available, including 
modification to existing formats to fit client specific needs, can be provided for a fee starting 
at $100.  Additional fees will be estimated by the lab and approved by the client.  Additional 
programming fees are billed at $50/hour.
PROJECT SPECIFICATIONS
Cancellation Fee - 
A fee will be charged for cancellation of samples/analyses after a project is received in the 
laboratory.  The fee will be based on the status of analysis at the time of cancellation in 
accordance with the following categories:
·      Received  - 35%
·      Prepped   - 50% 
·      Analyzed  - 95% 
  
Changes in Scope and Work Revisions - 
Project requirements must be agreed upon prior to sample receipt.  Samples will be logged 
according to the chain of custody received with the samples.  Changes after initiation of the 
project will be subject to additional charges, including labor time required to reset project, 
communicate changes to laboratory staff, and rework data.  Turnaround time will be reset or

Held Samples - 
·      Held samples not analyzed:  Samples submitted on hold will be billed at 35% of the 
analysis fee (minimum $10/sample).  If samples are later analyzed, the handling fee will be 
waived and only the analysis price will be charged.  Samples taken off hold with less than 
1/2 of the analytical hold time remaining may incur rush turnaround charges.  Samples will 
be disposed of 30 days after the report for analyzed samples in the same job is issued, 
unless alternate archival arrangements are made in advance.
·      Extracted/Prepped and Held samples: Samples submitted for prep and hold will be billed at 
60% of the analysis fee for each prepped sample (minimum $30/sample).  Samples taken 
off hold with less than 1/2 of the analytical hold time remaining may incur rush turnaround 
charges.  Samples will be disposed of 30 days after the report for analyzed samples in the 
same job is issued, unless alternate archival arrangements are made in advance.
·      Extended archival of samples:  Extended archival of samples (including held samples) may 
be available for a fee starting at $2 per container per month (minimum $10/sample).  This 
fee will be billed in advance on a quarterly basis for every quarter after the standard sample 
retention time of 30 days after the report is issued.  Fees for larger volumes, non-standard 
matrices or cold storage will be negotiated on a case-by-case basis.  Please contact your 
PM to inquire about availability and pricing for samples that are sent to the lab and 
archived. 
Matrix Spike/Spike Duplication (MS/MSD) Samples - 
When MS/MSDs are not specifically requested, Eurofins TestAmerica will strive to perform the 
required QC using whatever sample is available but will not report the QC results unless the 
client requests it.  The reporting of client requested MS/MSD results will be charged at 
applicable unit rates.  If MS/MSDs are specifically required or requested, the client must provide 
additional sample volume.
Multiple Dilutions Analyzed - 
Eurofins TestAmerica strives to analyze samples without dilution or with the minimum dilution 
required.  Samples are diluted to bring the primary analyte within the calibration range of the 
instrument, to compensate for matrix co-extractives, or to prevent instrument contamination.  
Eurofins TestAmerica will report the analytical run containing the highest concentration 
component/analyte in the sample within the calibrated (quantifiable) range of the method.   
Analytical screening runs are not reported.  If project specific data quality objectives require 
additional runs, analyses will be billable unless otherwise noted in this quote.  Please contact 
your PM to inquire about the availability of this service for your project.
Sample Disposal - 
Eurofins TestAmerica will dispose of non-hazardous samples, sample extracts and digestates 
30 days after the final report is issued.   Charges for disposal of non-routine or uniquely 
hazardous samples will be billed to the client.  Alternatively, samples can be returned to the 
client for disposal.  Cost of return shipping will be billable to the client.
Special Sample Handling Fees - 
Unit prices assume that samples are a single-phase matrix and that analyses can be performed

in accordance with the laboratory's standard analytical procedures.  If additional handling is

to):
·      Matrices requiring additional dilutions or special clean up steps
·      Multiphasic samples requiring separate preparations and/or analyses
·      Particle size reduction or special sub-sampling procedures   
·      Extra disposal costs for unique waste streams
Trip Blanks - 
Eurofins TestAmerica typically provides trip blanks with our sample kits containing volatile 
analysis. When samples are received at the laboratory with trip blanks, the lab will analyze, 
report and charge the unit rate for the analysis.  Please add this sample to your chain of 
custody.  If you do not want the trip blank analyzed, please note this on the COC.
  
ADDITIONAL BUSINESS TERMS AND CONDITIONS
Environmental Management Fee
If this fee is included in the Other Charges section of the pricing page, a fee equal to the listed 
percentage of the total invoice amount will be applied to all work done under this quotation.  
The Fee will appear as a separate line item on each invoice.   In the absence of any other firm 
pricing agreements, your sending work to us under this quotation will signify your acceptance of 
responsibility for payment of the Fee.
Field Parameters - 
pH, Temperature, and Dissolved Oxygen analyses, along with Residual Chlorine screening, are 
properly performed and treated in the field at the time of sample collection.   Laboratory 
analysis may result in a holding time exceedance qualifier.
Network or Subcontract Labs - 
·      Networking: Eurofins TestAmerica reserves the right to perform the services at any 
laboratory in the Eurofins TestAmerica network, unless the Client has required a particular 
location for the work.
·      Subcontracting: Eurofins TestAmerica reserves the right to subcontract services ordered by 
the Client to another laboratory or laboratories, if, in Eurofins TestAmerica's sole judgment, 
it is reasonably necessary, appropriate or advisable to do so.  Eurofins TestAmerica will 
make every effort to notify the client prior to delivering samples to an out-of-network 
laboratory.  Eurofins TestAmerica will in no way be liable for any subcontracted services 
(outside the Eurofins TestAmerica network) except for work performed at laboratories which 
have been audited and approved by Eurofins TestAmerica.
Price Surcharge Due To Sample Volume - 
Unless dictated by contract, this quotation is based on the scope of work defined in the quote 
request.  If the volume of samples submitted is less than 70% of the projected volume, a 
surcharge of 10% of the total project cost may be assessed.  
Professional and Administrative Services - 
A variety of professional and administrative services are available.  Prices for services not 
specifically detailed in this quotation will be billed in accordance with Eurofins TestAmerica's 
Professional Rate Schedule.
Taxes - 
Where reports are issued in or delivered to a state which assesses sales tax on Eurofins

EUROFINS TESTAMERICA TERMS AND CONDITIONS OF SALE (Short Form) 
 
 
© 2021, TestAmerica Laboratories, Inc., All Rights Reserved 
 
Std TC-1 (v11), Page 1 of 3 
When a purchaser (Client) places an order for laboratory, consulting or 
sampling services from TestAmerica Laboratories, Inc., d/b/a Eurofins 
TestAmerica a Delaware corporation, Eurofins TestAmerica shall provide the 
ordered services pursuant to these Terms and Conditions and the related 
Quotation or Price Schedule, or as agreed in a negotiated contract.  In the 
absence of a written agreement to the contrary, a client order constitutes an 
acceptance by the Client of Eurofins TestAmerica's offer to do business 
under these Terms and Conditions, and an agreement to be bound by these 
Terms and Conditions.  Receipt of a Client’s samples at a Eurofins 
TestAmerica laboratory constitutes acceptance of these Terms and 
Conditions (in the absence of any other negotiated contract).  No contrary or 
additional terms and conditions expressed in a Client's document shall be 
deemed to become a part of the contract created upon acceptance of these 
Terms and Conditions, unless accepted by Eurofins TestAmerica in writing. 
1.  
ORDERS AND RECEIPT OF SAMPLES 
1.1 A Client may place an order (i.e., specify a Scope of Work) either by 
submitting a purchase order to Eurofins TestAmerica in writing or by 
telephone subsequently confirmed in writing, or by negotiated contract.  
Whichever option the Client selects for placing an order, the order shall not 
be valid unless it contains sufficient specification to enable Eurofins 
TestAmerica to carry out the Client’s requirements.  In particular, samples 
must be accompanied by:  a) adequate instruction on type of analysis 
requested, and b) complete written disclosure of the known or suspected 
presence of any hazardous substances, as defined by applicable federal or 
state law.  If a Client fails to provide these required disclosures 
accompanying the submission of samples, and such failure results in an 
interruption in the lab’s ability to process work due to contamination of 
instruments or work areas, the Client will be responsible for the costs of 
clean-up and recovery.  
1.2 The Client shall provide one week’s advance notice of the sample 
delivery schedule, or any changes to the schedule, whenever possible. Upon 
timely delivery of samples, Eurofins TestAmerica will use its best efforts to 
meet mutually agreed turnaround times.  All turnaround times will be 
calculated from the point in time when Eurofins TestAmerica has determined 
that it can proceed with defined work following receipt, inspection of 
samples, and resolution of any discrepancies in Chain-of-Custody forms and 
project guidance regarding work to be done (Sample Delivery Acceptance).  
Rush turnaround times not requested in advance of the delivery of samples 
and specifically agreed to by the lab are not guaranteed.  If the Client 
changes the sample delivery schedule prior to Sample Delivery Acceptance, 
Eurofins TestAmerica reserves its rights to modify its turnaround time 
commitment, change the date upon which Eurofins TestAmerica will accept 
samples, or refuse Sample Delivery Acceptance for the affected samples. 
1.3 Eurofins TestAmerica reserves the right, exercisable at any time, to 
refuse or revoke Sample Delivery Acceptance for any sample which in the 
sole judgment of Eurofins TestAmerica: a) is of unsuitable volume; b) may 
pose a risk or become unsuitable for handling, transport, or processing for 
any health, safety, environmental or other reason, whether or not due to the 
presence of any hazardous substance in the sample and whether or not 
such presence has been disclosed to Eurofins TestAmerica by the Client; or 
c) holding times cannot be met, due to passage of more than 48 hours from 
the time of sampling or 1/2 the holding time for the requested test, whichever 
is less. 
1.4 Prior to Sample Delivery Acceptance, the entire risk of loss or damage 
to samples remains with the Client, except where Eurofins TestAmerica 
provides courier services.  In no event will Eurofins TestAmerica have any 
responsibility or liability for the action or inaction of any carrier shipping or 
delivering any sample to or from Eurofins TestAmerica's premises.  Client is 
responsible for assuring that any sample that contains or may contain any 
hazardous substance to be delivered to Eurofins TestAmerica's premises is 
properly packaged, labeled, transported and delivered, all in accordance 
with applicable laws. 
1.5 Eurofins TestAmerica reserves the right to begin processing samples 
upon receipt, unless the Client specifically notifies Eurofins TestAmerica in 
writing prior to sample receipt that the samples are to be held without 
preparation or other processing or pending receipt of a purchase order.  
Eurofins TestAmerica shall under no circumstances be responsible for 
missed holding times or turnaround times or for re-sampling costs if samples 
are released from hold with less than 48 hours or 1/2 the holding time for the 
requested test remaining, whichever is less.  
2.  
PAYMENT TERMS 
2.1 Services performed by Eurofins TestAmerica will be in accordance with 
prices quoted and later confirmed in writing or as stated in the Price 
Schedule.  Quoted prices do not include sales tax.  Applicable sales tax will 
be added to invoices where required by law. 
2.2 Invoices may be submitted to Client upon completion of any sample 
delivery group.  Billing corrections must be requested within 30 days of 
invoice date.  Payment in advance is required for all clients except those 
whose credit has been established with Eurofins TestAmerica.  For clients 
with approved credit, payment terms are net 30 days from the date of invoice 
by Eurofins TestAmerica, unless alternative terms have been agreed in a 
separate written agreement.  Payment shall be made without retainage, and 
shall not be contingent upon the receipt of funds from third parties.  All 
overdue payments are subject to an additional interest and service charge of 
one and one half percent (1.5%) (or the maximum rate permissible by law, 
whichever is less) per month or portion thereof from the due date until the 
date of payment.  All fees are charged or billed directly to the Client.  The 
billing of a third party will not be accepted without a statement, signed by the 
third party, acknowledging and accepting payment responsibility in 
accordance with these payment terms.   
2.3 If Client fails to make timely payment of its invoices, Eurofins 
TestAmerica reserves the right to pursue all appropriate remedies, including 
withdrawing certifications, suspending work and withholding delivery of data 
under this order without recourse.  Client shall be responsible for all 
reasonable fees, expenses, and costs of collection including but not limited 
to arbitrator's and attorney’s fees.  Eurofins TestAmerica reserves the right 
to refuse to proceed with work at any time based upon an unfavorable Client 
credit report. 
3.  
CHANGE ORDERS, TERMINATION 
3.1 Changes to the Scope of Work, price, or result delivery date may be 
initiated by Eurofins TestAmerica after Sample Delivery Acceptance due to 
any condition which conflicts with analytical, QA or other protocols warranted 
in these Terms and Conditions.  Eurofins TestAmerica will not proceed with 
such changes until an agreement with the Client is reached on the amount of 
any cost, schedule change or technical change to the Scope of Work, and 
such agreement is documented in writing. 
3.2 Changes to the Scope of Work, including but not limited to increasing 
or decreasing the work, changing test and analysis specification, or 
acceleration in the performance of the work may be initiated by the Client 
after Sample Delivery Acceptance.  Such change must be documented in 
writing and may result in a change in cost and turnaround time commitment.  
Eurofins TestAmerica's acceptance of such changes is contingent upon 
technical feasibility and operational capacity. 
3.3 Suspension or termination of all or any part of the work may be initiated 
by the Client.  Eurofins TestAmerica will be compensated consistent with 
Section 2 of these Terms and Conditions.  Eurofins TestAmerica will 
complete all work in progress and be paid in full for all work completed. 
4.  
WARRANTIES AND LIABILITY  
4.1 Where applicable, Eurofins TestAmerica will use appropriate and 
approved analytical test methods.  Eurofins TestAmerica has referenced 
these methods in its Laboratory Quality Manuals and has documented them 
in Standard Operating Procedures.  Eurofins TestAmerica reserves the right 
based on its reasonable judgment to deviate from these methodologies as 
necessary or appropriate to the extent required by the nature or composition

with recognized standards of the industry and/or Eurofins TestAmerica's 
Laboratory Quality Manuals.  Client may request that Eurofins TestAmerica 
perform according to a mutually agreed Quality Assurance Project Plan 
(QAPP).  If samples arrive prior to agreement on a QAPP, Eurofins 
TestAmerica will proceed with analyses under its standard Quality Manuals 
then in effect.  Eurofins TestAmerica will not be responsible for any 
resampling or other charges if work must be repeated to comply with a 
subsequently finalized QAPP. 
4.2 Eurofins TestAmerica shall start preparation and/or analysis within 
holding times provided that Sample Delivery Acceptance occurs within 48 
hours of sampling or 1/2 of the holding time for the test, whichever is less,

EXHIBIT A
SCOPE OF WORK
This Exhibit acts as a supplemental information document and is made a part of the Professional
Services Agreement (Agreement) entered into by and between the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming (“City”)
and Trihydro Corporation (“Consultant”). The provisions of the Exhibit shall be incorporated into the
Agreement. The Consultant’s proposal shall be attached as a reference and shall be considered
subsidiary to this agreement.
A.1
GROUNDWATER SAMPLING AND ANALYSIS
Consultant shall perform environmental monitoring as described in the April 12, 2017 Sanitary Landfill
Environmental Monitoring Plan (SLEMP), prepared by Trihydro Corporation. The Consultant will follow
all the steps outlined in the SLEMP section 2.3 Groundwater Monitoring Procedures. As stated in SLEMP
section 2.3.2.1 Well Evacuation using a dedicated pump, eight (8) of the wells in the Semi-Confined
Aquifer have dedicated pneumatically driven pumps. The City does own, and the Consultant will have
access to, a towed compressor but the compressor should not be considered reliable and the Consultant
is responsible for obtaining the samples if the City owned compressor is not working. All other wells will
be sampled using bailers. Table 3 of the SLEMP outlines the sampling schedule.
Sampling shall be performed in the field on the following months:
1)

A.2
GROUNDWATER DATA STATISTICAL ANALYSES
Consultant shall follow the statistical analysis outlined in the SLEMP section 4.0 Groundwater Data
Statistical Analyses. The Consultant will receive the Groundwater monitoring results back to September
1982 once the Professional Services Agreement has been signed by both parties.
A.3
FLUID GAUGING
Consultant shall perform groundwater and leachate gauging for the wells, sumps and other locations as
outlined in the SLEMP Table 4. Fluid gauging shall be performed in the field every sampling event,
beginning April 2018, and continuing through and including August 2020.

A.4
EXPANSION WELL BASELINE MONITORING
As a variance condition for the expansion of the Landfill the City has agreed to install and monitor four
(4) new monitoring wells. The construction of these wells may or may not be constructed during this
contract, but in the event the wells are constructed during this contract period, the Consultant will
establish a WDEQ approved monitoring plan and perform eight (8) quarterly sampling events as outlined
in the SLEMP section 2.4 Future Expansion Area Wells. A second variance condition that needs to be met
is outlined in the SLEMP section 2.5 Off-site Water Well Monitoring Program. Table 8 of the SLEMP
identifies the known off-site water wells that will need to be tested. Once the City authorizes the
Consultant to enact the off-site monitoring outlined in the SLEMP section 2.5.1 Baseline Monitoring
Program, the Consultant will create and submit a sampling protocol to the WDEQ prior to initiating the
first sampling event.
A.5
METHANE MONITORING
Consultant shall perform methane gas monitoring in the Construction Demolition Recycling Facility,
Landfill Equipment Shelter Shop Area and methane monitoring wells (construction date to be
determined) as outlined in the referenced SLEMP. There will be fourteen (14) methane monitoring wells
surrounding the permitted boundary of the Landfill. Methane monitoring shall be performed in the field
during the following months:

A.6
IMMEDIATE NOTIFICATION
Consultant shall immediately, upon becoming aware of any one or more of the following, notify the City
by phone call and written, hand delivered letter if:
1)
Methane concentration above 25% Lower Explosion Limit (LEL) in facility buildings or above
100% LEL at compliance points at the landfill boundary.
2)
Groundwater detection above Groundwater Protection Standards.
3)
Statistically significant increases in contaminant concentrations in groundwater.

All requirements are explained in the SLEMP section 6.0 Notification Requirements.
A.7
EVENT REPORTS
Consultant shall submit a draft copy (electronic is acceptable) of a summary event report as outlined in
section 7.0 Reporting Requirements of the SLEMP within 30-days of receipt of complete and accurate
analytical results for each April and August monitoring events. Consultant shall incorporate City
comments and submit one paper and one electronic copy of the final report (if paper draft, page
changes are acceptable) to the City and paper and electronic deliverables to WDEQ per their
requirements not later than 30-days following submission of draft.
A.8
ANNUAL REPORTS
Consultant shall submit a draft copy (electronic is acceptable) of a comprehensive annual report to the
City by March 1 of each contract year. Each annual report shall be outlined in Section 7.0 Reporting
Requirements of the SLEMP. Consultant shall incorporate City comments and submit one electronic and
one paper copy of final report (if paper draft, page changes are acceptable) to the City and paper and
electronic deliverables to WDEQ per their requirements by April 1 of each contract year.

1.0
INTRODUCTION
This Sanitary Landfill Environmental Monitoring Plan (SLEMP) has been prepared to address groundwater, leachate,
and methane monitoring requirements at the City of Cheyenne Sanitary Landfill, also known as the Happy Jack
Landfill (Landfill). The plan has been prepared as part of the facility’s 2017 lifetime permit application, primarily to
fulfill monitoring requirements contained within the Wyoming Department of Environmental Quality/Solid and
Hazardous Waste Division (WDEQ/SHWD) municipal solid waste landfill regulations. Additional monitoring
requirements have also been incorporated pursuant to the WDEQ November 25, 2015 Location Standards Variance
Approval for the expansion area. This SLEMP has been prepared in accordance with revised Chapter 2 of the
WDEQ/SHWD Rules and Regulations dated January 3, 2017.
The primary objective of the environmental monitoring program at the Landfill is early identification of impacts to the
subsurface as a result of landfill activities. Additionally, the hydrogeologic and analytical data generated during routine
monitoring may be used for assessment and/or remedial design purposes, if impacts are identified in subsurface soils or
groundwater beneath the landfill.
The remainder of the SLEMP is organized into the following sections:
Section 2.0— Groundwater and Leachate Monitoring Plan: provides a description for each of the aquifers beneath
the landfill, leachate monitoring points, different phases of monitoring, frequency of sampling, as well as fluid
gauging and groundwater sampling procedures. This section also addresses the off-site water well monitoring
program for wells within a half mile of the expansion area boundary that are permitted or certificated for domestic
or stock use.
•
Section 3.0— Groundwater Data Quality Assurance/Quality Control: presents the quality assurance objectives for
this project and the methodology for reviewing field and laboratory data.
•
Section 4.0— Groundwater Data Statistical Analyses: includes a description of the statistical methods used to
evaluate the groundwater analytical results from each monitoring event.
•
Section 5.0
— Landfill Methane Monitoring Plan: describes the monitoring system, frequency, and procedures for
monitoring methane in landfill structures and in the subsurface.
•
Section 6.0
— Notification Requirements: outlines the notification requirements and follow up actions to
statistically significant increases in contaminant concentrations in groundwater, detections above groundwater
protection standards, and methane concentration measuring more than 25% or 100% of the lower explosive limit
(LEL).

•
Section 7.0
— Reporting Requirements: details the frequency and content of the environmental monitoring reports
submitted to the WDEQ/SHWD.
•
Section 8.0
— References.

2.0
GROUNDWATER AND LEACHATE MONITORING PLAN
A total of 32 groundwater monitoring wells/piezometers are in the current groundwater monitoring network at the
Landfill. In addition, 4 leachate wells (installed in the southern portion of the landfill at the refuse-soil interface), and 5
leachate sumps (installed in the northern trenches) are currently in the leachate monitoring program. Figure 1 shows
the locations of each of the groundwater monitoring wells and piezometers, as well as the leachate wells and sumps.
Hydrogeologic zones of completion are noted on Figure 1 where applicable.
Table I provides a summary of the construction history of monitoring wells and piezorneters installed at the Landfill,
dating back to 1975, including a list of associated reference documents. Construction details for each of the active
monitoring wells and piezometers is included in Table 2 with the available associated boring logs/construction
diagrams included in Attachment A. Each well and piezometer at the landfill was constructed with a protective surface
casing and locked to prevent tampering.
2.1
PHASES OF GROUNDWATER MONITORING
Groundwater monitoring is divided into two phases at the Landfill: detection and assessment monitoring, following the
general guidelines established in Chapter 2 of the WDEQ/SHWD Rules and Regulations (WDEQ 2017). Routine
groundwater monitoring including gauging and sampling activities will be conducted in April and August of each year.
Tables 3 and 4 present summaries of the sampling and fluid level monitoring plans for this landfill. Tables 5, 6, and 7
show the Solid Waste Rules and Regulations (SWRR) Chapter 2 Appendix A, Appendix B, and Appendix C
Constituents, as required for detection and/or assessment monitoring.
2.1.1
DETECTION MONITORING
Detection monitoring includes the collection and analysis of groundwater samples where statistically significant
impacts to groundwater have not been identified. Detection monitoring requires the analysis of a select list of
constituents, followed by statistical analysis of the results to determine if there are increasing contaminant
concentrations above background or baseline water quality. Groundwater samples collected during detection
monitoring are analyzed for WDEQ/SHWD Appendix A (Table 5) and Appendix C (Table 7) constituents.
For routine detection monitoring, samples will be analyzed for Appendix A constituents on an annual basis and for
Appendix C constituents on a semi-annual basis. However, for new monitoring well installations (see Section 2.4),
baseline data will be developed by collecting samples quarterly for the first two years. Samples will be analyzed for
M \StoV\SWPot
708_CheyenneSLEMP_REV-CLEAN_RPTdocx
2-1

Revised January 30, 2018
both Appendix A and C constituents at each sampling event. After the first two years, the sampling schedule will
return to routine detection monitoring as described above.
2.1.2
ASSESSMENT MONITORING
Assessment monitoring is implemented whenever a statistically significant increase over background water quality has
been detected for an Appendix A constituent, indicating potential impacts to groundwater quality. Assessment
monitoring specifies a much broader range of analyses within selected wells to characterize the nature of groundwater
impacts. Historically, a subset of wells developed in consultation with the WDEQ Administrator were sampled under
assessment monitoring and analyzed for Appendix B (Table 6) constituents at least annually.
When an Appendix B or C constituent is detected at a statistically significant level above the applicable groundwater
protection standard for that constituent, additional monitoring wells may be required to characterize the nature and
extent of the release. The EE-series wells and PIE-9 are used to help define the nature and extent of VOC impacts first
identified in compliance monitoring well SRM-6. Historically, samples collected from these wells were analyzed
annually for the WDEQ/SHWD Appendix A constituent list plus the constituent dichiorodifluoromethane from the
Appendix B list. Dichlorodifluoromethane is no longer sampled alongside Appendix A constituents; the current
monitoring plan does not deviate from constituents listed in WDEQ/SHWD Appendices A, B, and C.
All wells scheduled for assessment monitoring (the subset of compliance wells described above) will be sampled
semiannually for Appendix C parameters and annually for Appendix B (VOCs and Inorganics). This group of wells
also includes up-gradient wells in the Perched Water Bearing Zone and the Principal Unconfined Aquifer for statistical
comparison. The assessment monitoring wells will be sampled for a reduced list of Appendix B constituents (only
VOCs and Inorganics) based on an extensive data record that showed no significant detections of SVOCs or pesticides,
herbicides or PCBs. The down-gradient wells scheduled for assessment monitoring will be sampled once every five
years for the full Appendix B list, to confirm the lack of detection of these constituents.
The City will collect and analyze samples for the Appendix B (VOCs and Inorganics) constituent list during the April
sampling event. However, if there is insufficient liquid to complete the analyses, a second attempt will be made during
the August event. Table 3 shows which wells are scheduled for assessment monitoring and Table 6 presents the
Appendix B constituent list.
2.2
GROUNDWATER BEARING ZONES, AQUIFERS AND LEACHATE MONITORING POINTS
Four (4) saturated units have been identified beneath the Landfill. These include two (2) Perched Water Bearing
Zones, the Principal Unconfined Aquifer, and the Semi-Confined (formerly called Confined) Aquifer. The perched
2-2
708_CheyenneSLEMPREV2-CLEAN_RPT - Copydocx

Revised January 30, 2018
zones and the aquifers are not considered to be hydraulically connected beneath the Landfill. These water bearing
zones and aquifers and the associated monitoring networks are described briefly in the following sections. Leachate
wells are screened across the refuse-soil interface and are utilized to measure and characterize leachate generated
beneath the unlined areas of the landfill. Leachate sumps are also monitored to assess the volume and characteristics of
leachate generated from the lined areas. Additional monitoring wells, sumps, and piezometers may be installed at the
landfill as part of investigation and monitoring activities.
2.2.1
PERCHED I WATER BEARING ZONE
The Perched 1 Water Bearing Zone has been identified as the uppermost water-bearing unit beneath the landfill.
Intermittent saturated conditions have been encountered in the Spring Creek alluvium along the southern portions of the
landfill extending into the Ogallala Formation to the east of Well SRM-6. Depth to water in the Perched I Water
Bearing Zone in the southern portions of the landfill ranges between six (6) and 45 feet below ground surface (ft-bgs).
East of the landfill, the perched zone is encountered at depths between 55 and 75 ft-bgs, and is limited at depth by a
sandy clay layer in the Ogallala Formation. This zone is of limited extent both horizontally and vertically. Flow
through the Perched I Water Bearing Zone is likely the result of channelized subsurface flow such as a sandy braided
stream depositional environment.
In order to limit the amount of water entering the landfill from the Spring Creek drainage, a cut-off wall was
constructed near the southern boundary where the creek enters the landfill. In addition, Spring Creek was re-routed to
the south of the landfill. As part of that project, a pump was installed in an up-slope well to dewater any saturated
alluvium in front of the cut-off wall. These efforts appear to be successful with minimal amounts of water entering the
landfill through leakage around the cut-off wall or vertical infiltration through the landfill cap. In fact, many of the
wells/piezometers screened across the Perched I Water Bearing Zone only intermittently contain groundwater, with
limited water level data often precluding delineation of potentiometric surface mapping.
The Perched I monitoring wells will be gauged in April and August, of each year (Table 4). Monitoring wells and
piezometers screened in the Perched I Water Bearing Zone are sampled under either assessment or detection
monitoring, as outlined below and in Table 3:
Detection Monitoring: Piezometers P-I, P-3, P-4, P-5, P-8, P-10, P-Il and Well S-9 are scheduled to be sampled
for the Appendix A and C constituent lists in April and for the Appendix C list in August of each year.
•
Assessment Monitoring: Groundwater samples are collected from Well SRM-6, PIE-9, EE-04 and EE-07 and
analyzed for the Appendix B (VOCs and Inorganics) and Appendix C lists in April, and the Appendix C list in
August of each year.

2-3

Revised January 30, 2018
The Perched 1 Water Bearing Zone is generally referred to as the Perched Water Bearing Zone (without numbering)
throughout the documentation post-2003, since wells in the Perched 2 Water Bearing Zone were abandoned in 2003 as
described below.
2.2.2
PERCHED 2 WATER BEARING ZONE
A Perched 2 Water Bearing Zone was identified in two Wells EE-01 and EE-02 installed along the eastern edge of the
landfill. Monitoring Wells EE-01 and EE-02 were plugged and abandoned on June 9, 2003, because refuse was
scheduled to be placed in close proximity to these two wells due to completion of planned development of the waste
disposal area approved by the WDEQ/SHWD. The monitoring wells were plugged and abandoned in accordance with
the provisions of Chapter 26, Section 11 of the Water Quality Division Rules and Regulations. The concrete pads were
destroyed and the protective casings removed. Exposed portions of the well casings were cut off below the ground
surface. The wells were then sealed with a bentonite grout from the total depth of the well to ground surface. A
permanent marking was set into a concrete surface monument indicating the well identification and date of
abandonment. The abandonment notices filed with the Wyoming State Engineer’s Office were included in the May
through August Environmental Monitoring Report submitted to the WDEQ/SHWD on October 28, 2003.
2.2.3
PRINCIPAL UNCONFINED AQUIFER
The entire landfill is underlain by an unconfined aquifer approximately 150 -160 ft-bgs. Thickness in the Principal
Unconfined Aquifer varies from five (5) to 50 feet; groundwater flows from the southwest to the northeast at a gradient
of approximately 0.01 to 0.02 feet per foot.
There are twelve (12) monitoring wells screened across the Principal Unconfined Aquifer at the landfill. Fluid levels
from each of the wells screened in the Principal Unconfined Aquifer will be gauged in April, and August each year
(Table 4). These wells will be sampled following the detection and/or assessment phase of groundwater monitoring, as
outlined below and in Table 3:
•
Detection Monitoring: Wells PU-I, PU-2, PU-3, PU-4, PU-5, and P3-i, P3-2 and P3-3 are scheduled to be
sampled for the Appendix A and C constituent lists in April and for the Appendix C list in August each year.
•
Assessment Monitoring: Groundwater samples will be collected and analyzed from Wells SRM-4, SRM-5, SRM-7
and SRM-8 for the Appendix B (VOCs and Inorganics) and Appendix C constituent lists in April, and the
Appendix C list in August each year.

Revised August 23, 2017
2.2.4
SEMI-CONFINED AQUIFER
In 2013, a series of groundwater monitoring wells (GMW series) was installed to help define the subsurface
hydrogeology specifically in the landfill expansion area. WDEQ asked for confirmation as to whether the GMW-series
wells are confined and if they monitor the same water bearing zone as the C-series wells, a set of wells that had been
described as monitoring a confined zone within the Ogallala Aquifer identified when the wells were drilled in 1999.
The confined zone was said to be primarily composed of sand with minor amounts of clay, silt, and gravel which lies
below the Principal Unconfined Aquifer unit at approximately 170 ft-bgs.
A transducer study was then completed to shed more light on the local subsurface hydrology as a condition of the
WDEQ Location Standards Variance Approval. The results of the study indicated that the Confined Aquifer would be
more appropriately called a Semi-Confined aquifer. The wells monitor different intervals but may represent a series of
“stacked” water bearing zones that locally are connected to varying degrees. Some may meet the definition of confined
(C-series) because water levels in certain wells are consistently above the screened zone, but the wells as a group do
not appear to represent confined aquifer conditions. The Ogallala Aquifer in this part of the country is very complex
and the degree of interconnectivity is difficult to accurately assess. It was recommended that the GMW and C series
wells be plotted together to represent groundwater flow conditions below the Principal Unconfined Aquifer set of
wells. USGS mapping shows a general northeasterly groundwater flow gradient that begins to change to the northwest
consistent with what is also observed in the potentiometric surface maps constructed based on the C-series and
GMW-series wells.
Approximately ten (10) municipal water wells operated by the Cheyenne Board of Public Utilities (BOPU) are located
within a one-mile radius of the landfill site. These wells are installed at depths between 200 and 400 ft-bgs. Pumping
from the wells operated by the Board of Public Utilities is intermittent as necessary to augment the City of Cheyenne’s
water supply. Each of the water supply wells has the capacity to produce between 150 to 600 gallons per minute from
the Ogallala Aquifer. The transducer study results indicated that even at high late-summer pumping rates, the BOPU
pumping wells did not alter the aquifer flow direction from the regional flow pattern. The Cheyenne Board of Public
Utilities intermittently collects groundwater samples from these wells and analyzes the samples for the Appendix A
constituent list. This data is provided to the City and shared with WDEQ through the annual report. Three (3) C-series
and 5 GMW-series wells will be gauged and sampled in April and August of each year according to the detection
monitoring program (Tables 3 and 4).
7O8_SLEMP_REV1\1_Text2O1 708_CheyenneSLEMP_REV-CLEAN_RPTdocx
2-5

Revised August 23, 2017
2.2.5
LEACHATE WELLS
There are four active leachate wells screened into the refuse across the southern portions of the Landfill. Figure 1
shows the locations of each of the leachate wells. Historically, water had been sporadically present in over 11 leachate
wells, based on fluid gauging results in April and August of each year. In the past, grab samples had been collected
annually from Leachate Wells PIE-I, S-7A, and S-4 and analyzed for the Appendix A constituent list in order to
quantify constituent concentrations detected in the leachate along the southern portion of the landfill. Constituent
concentrations are primarily for information purposes to characterize the leachate quality and are not related to any
compliance criteria. The City has been collecting leachate data for many years and additional sampling was not likely
to provide added value. As a result, the four active leachate wel Is (P1 E- 1, S-i, S-4 and S-7A) are not being sampled
but fluid levels are gauged during each sampling event (Table 4).
2.2.6
LEACHATE SUMPS
There are five (5) sumps installed to monitor and evacuate leachate generated beneath the northern trenches at the
landfill. Figure
1 shows the locations of each of the leachate sumps installed at the landfill. Fluid levels in sumps
NT-I and NT-2 are manually gauged semi-annually in concert with the routine sampling events to the nearest 0.1-foot
relative to the top of each sump. NT-i and NT-2 are not pumped unless leachate depths approach 12-inches of
accumulation above the liner. Fluid levels in these two sumps have been either dry or declining such that future
pumping is unlikely.
Fluid levels in NT-3 through NT-5 are recorded using pressure transducers installed in each sump. Landfill staff record
fluid levels and leachate pumping volumes read from the electronic display on a monthly basis. The pressure
transducers in Sumps NT-3 through NT-S will be verified every two years. The pressure transducers will be verified to
the nearest 0.1-foot utilizing a water level indicator. If the measurement between the water level indicator and the
pressure transducer differ by greater than 0.3 foot, the transducer will be recalibrated. The sumps are evacuated using
dedicated submersible pumps programmed to prevent more than 12-inches of leachate from accumulating above the
cell liner. Grab samples will be collected annually from the NT-5 leachate sump (if sufficient volume if present) and
analyzed for the Appendix B (VOC) and C constituent lists in order to quantify constituent concentrations detected in
the leachate (Table 3). Sump NT-5 was selected because it collects leachate from the most recently constructed cell
and will produce leachate for the longest future time period. In addition, sample collection can be accomplished easily
because of a sample port location on the discharge line and no confined space entry is required. Again, constituent
concentrations are primarily for information purposes to characterize the leachate quality and are not related to any
compliance criteria. As new landfill cells are constructed, the City proposes to sample one leachate collection area for
the new cells for Appendix A and C constituents annually.

2.3
GROUNDWATER MONITORING PROCEDURES
This section details the procedures and techniques that will be followed for groundwater monitoring at the Landfill.
Due to the heterogeneity of the hydrogeologic units encountered beneath the landfill, it may be necessary to modify
these procedures as sampling progresses to ensure that representative groundwater samples are collected and submitted
to the laboratory for analysis.
2.3.1
FLUID LEVEL MONITORING
Upon arriving at a well location, sampling personnel will check the well for damage and remove the lock and well cap.
Any damage to the wellhead or casing, or any items requiring maintenance will be noted in the field sampling record
and reported to the project manager so that the necessary repairs can be scheduled. Field personnel will wear new,
sterile gloves during all preparation and sampling activities.
Fluid levels within each water bearing zone will be measured within a 24-hour period during the two scheduled
sampling events (April and August). Fluid level measurements will be performed using a water level indicator accurate
to 0.01-feet. The fluid level probe will be lowered down the center of the well casing. When the probe encounters
water, an audio alarm is triggered. The measurement will be made from the pre-marked (surveyed) measuring point on
the north side of the well casing. Manufacturer’s instructions will be followed to ensure proper care of the fluid level
probe. The total depth of the well will be measured annually to determine if the well is silting in and needs to be
rehabilitated or replaced.
If non-aqueous phase liquid (NAPL) is observed in any well, the thickness will be measured with an interface probe. A
typical interface probe gives an audible beeping sound when submerged in water and a solid tone when submerged in
NAPL. The fluid level measurements will be recorded on the fluid level summary form (or similar) included in
Attachment B.
All measurements during a particular monitoring event are made to the reference points with the same instrument, to
ensure internal consistency. The vertical reference points (i.e. north side top of casing) are established by a licensed
surveyor or under supervision of a professional engineer in relation to the nearest permanent benchmark. The
horizontal well coordinates are established by marking distances in reference to the project site plan in triangulation or
by surveying in reference to the nearest permanent benchmark. The exposed portion of the tape and the probe will be
decontaminated before performing measurements at each monitoring well.

Revised August 23, 2017
2.3.2
WELL EVACUATION PROCEDURES
Depending on the depth to water and the yield of a well, a dedicated pneumatic pump with dedicated tubing or a
disposable bailer will be employed to purge groundwater from selected wells and piezometers. Field personnel shall
note the approximate evacuation rate on the groundwater sampling forms (Attachment B).
Conventional sampling techniques will be used to evacuate and sample monitoring wells screened in the Perched Water
Bearing Zone, as well as the leachate wells and sumps. The column of standing fluid in each well (casing volume) will
be calculated based on the measured fluid level, total well depth, and well diameter. Where possible, the calculated
well casing volume will be evacuated from the well a minimum of three times. Some wells may be pumped or bailed
“dry” before three casing volumes are evacuated. If this occurs, groundwater samples will be collected following
sufficient recharge to the well.
2.3.2.1
WELL EVACUATION USING A DEDICATED PUMP
There are currently eight wells with dedicated pumps at this facility. This includes pumps at the Semi-Confined
Aquifer Wells C-2 and C-3 as well as pumps at the Principal Unconfined Aquifer Wells PU-I, PU-2, SRM-4, SRM-5,
SRM-6, and SRM-8. These pumps are operated at speeds varying from 0.19 gpm to 0.29 gpm during evacuation and
then allowed to pump a minimum of three well casing volumes. This is done by using an air compressor and discharge
controller in collaboration with the dedicated pumps. Wells that have had low rechargeability during previous events
will be purged to the extent possible; however, enough volume will be left in the well to ensure sufficient water is
available to fill the laboratory required volume for analyses. The only well likely affected by this scenario is SRM-5
and will be determined through the volume of water in the well and recharge rate during evacuation.
2.3.2.2
GROUNDWATER EVACUATION USING BAILERS
Some wells will be evacuated using bailers due to the minimal volume of groundwater in the well. When collecting
groundwater using a bailer, the bailer will be slowly lowered using twine or cable into the well to minimize agitation of
the water table. The bailer will be raised to the surface manually or using an electric or battery operated auto bailer
downrigger system. Wells that require an auto bailer downrigger system are C-I, GMW-03, GMW-04, GMW-06, and
GMW-07. Disposable polyethylene bailers will be used to purge wells. Wells P3-I, P3-3, PU-3, and PIE-I have had
low rechargeability during previous events and will be bailed to the extent possible; however, enough volume will be
left in the well to ensure sufficient water is available to fill the laboratory required volume for analyses.
