2.3.2.3
ALTERNATIVE SAMPLING TECHNIQUES
In the future, the City of Cheyenne may opt to perform the sampling at the landfill using alternative sampling
techniques. At this time, no decision has been made as to the alternative method or methods that will be used. If the
City of Cheyenne decides to use an alternative sampling method in the future, detailed sampling methods will be
submitted to the WDEQ/SHWD prior to any changes in sampling techniques at the landfill. The following is one
alternative sampling techniques that may be considered by the City of Cheyenne:
•
A low-flow “minimal drawdown” sampling methodology to evacuate groundwater and sample wells screened in
the Principal Unconfined and the Confined Aquifers. Low-flow sampling involves removing water at the same
rate as it flows through the formation minimizing the entrainment of sediment. Low-flow sampling will conform
to USEPA recommended procedures outlined in the document, Low-Flow (Minimal Drawdown) Groundwater
Sampling Procedures (Puls and Barcelona, 1995). The pumps would be installed so that the intake height is
located in the interval of interest as determined in conjunction with the City of Cheyenne and the WDEQ/SHWD.
2.3.3
FIELD ANALYSES
Field parameters including specific conductance, pH, and temperature will be measured during groundwater evacuation
and at the completion of sample collection. Field parameters will be recorded on the groundwater sampling form for
each well. Physical characteristics of the groundwater will be noted and recorded during sampling (e.g. sediment,
hydrocarbon sheen, color, odor, etc.).
2.3.4
SAMPLE WITHDRAWAL
Groundwater samples will be collected from wells using dedicated pump/tubing or from disposable bailers. In
accordance with SWRR Chapter 2, Section 9.b.i.C.lI, no samples will be field filtered prior to laboratory analysis.
Sample agitation will be minimized during sampling and groundwater will be transferred directly from the sampling
device into the sample containers, intermediary containers will not be used for sample collection. Sample bottles shall
be filled as appropriate to minimize headspace. Volatile organic analysis (VOA) sample vials will be filled with zero
headspace. Field personnel will wear disposable gloves to prevent contamination of samples and equipment.
Groundwater samples will be submitted for analysis in accordance with the WDEQ/SHWD Appendix A list (Table 5),
which includes VOCs by USEPA Method 8260B and total metals via USEPA Method 6020 or the Appendix B list
(Table 6) which includes additional VOCs and total metals, as well as, semi-volatile organic compounds (SVOCs) by
USEPA Method 8270C; and pesticides, herbicides/fungicides, and polychlorinated biphenyls (PCBs) via USEPA
Methods 8081 A, 8151 A, and 8082, respectively. In addition, the City of Cheyenne will monitor the Appendix C list
(Table 7) of geochemical parameters from each of the wells included in the sampling network.

A trip blank prepared by the laboratory will be shipped with the groundwater samples. The samples will be labeled and

2.3.5
FIELD MEASUREMENTS, CALIBRATION, AND DECONTAMINATION
Field instruments will be used to analyze air and/or water samples during monitoring activities at the landfill. Field
measurements provide information critical to assessing health and safety issues, evaluating the nature, degree, and
extent of impacts, and ensuring representative samples are selected for laboratory analyses. Therefore, calibration and
maintenance of field instruments is important to achieving the overall project objectives.
Numerous types and brands of field instruments are currently available for use during environmental assessment
projects. Maintenance and calibration procedures vary depending on the type, manufacturer, model, and age of the
instruments. Therefore, specifying calibration and maintenance procedures for one or many types of instruments is not
considered to be practicable in the SLEMP.
Instruments and equipment used to gather, generate, or measure environmental data will be calibrated with sufficient
frequency and in a manner that is consistent with the manufacturers’ specifications. A record of calibration will be
maintained documenting calibration results and will include the date, standards, personnel, and calibration results.
Sampling equipment and containers will be decontaminated prior to sampling, either in the laboratory and/or in the
field. The sampling devices will be decontaminated in the field immediately prior to and after sampling each site. In
general, decontamination will consist of a detergent/water wash, tap water rinse, and distilled water final rinse followed

2.3.6
DOCUMENTATION REQUIREMENTS
All field activities and samples collected will be documented in order to verify compliance with the SLEMP. Field
forms will be used to document the monitoring activities. Information generated from sampling activities will be
documented on the appropriate forms including:
•
Fluid Level Summary
•
Groundwater Sampling Form

Incoming documents will be dated and filed. If distribution is necessary, the documents will be distributed to project
personnel. Examples of all of these forms are included in Attachment B.
2.3.7
CHAIN-OF-CUSTODY
A sample is under custody if it is in the field personnel’s possession or if the sample was in the field personnel’s
possession and then locked up, or placed in a designated secure area. The following procedures will be used in the
field:
1.
The field personnel will be responsible for the care and custody of the samples collected until they are delivered to
the analytical laboratory or entrusted carrier. As few people as possible will handle the samples.
2.
Field forms will be used to document the data collection activities such as the date, start and end time of activities,
field personnel name, weather conditions, sample location, equipment used to collect sample, depth of sample, time
of collection, sample description, sample identification number, and the volume and number of containers. Entries
will be made in ink with no erasures. A single stroke will be used to cross out incorrect information; corrections
will be dated and initialed.
3.
A chain-of-custody will be completed in the field to the fullest extent possible prior to sample shipment. It will
include the following information: sample number, time collected, source of sample and location, depth from
which sample was taken, preservative, and name of field personnel. The chain-of-custody will be filled out in a
legible manner, using ink, and signed by field personnel. Similar information will be provided on the sample label,

2.3.8
CUSTODY FOR SHIPMENT
The following procedures will be used when transferring custody of samples:
1.
Samples will always be accompanied by a chain-of-custody completed according to the protocols outlined in this
section. When transferring samples, the individuals relinquishing and receiving them will sign, indicate the date,
and note the time on the chain-of-custody. Upon arrival at the laboratory, internal custody procedures will be
followed, in accordance with standard laboratory procedures.
2.
Samples will be packaged properly for shipment and dispatched to the laboratory for analysis with individual
custody records accompanying each shipment. Shipping containers will be sealed for shipment to the laboratory.
The method of shipment, courier name, and other pertinent information will be entered in the remarks section of
the chain-of-custody.
3.
All shipments will be accompanied by the chain-of-custody form identifying the contents. The original record will
accompany the shipment, and a copy will be retained by the field sampler.
4.
Proper documentation will be maintained for shipments by the common carrier.
2.3.9
SAMPLE SHIPMENT PROCEDURES
Sample containers will be packed securely in a cooler to minimize the potential for breakage during shipment. Packing
materials, such as poly-foam or bubble wrap, shall be used to protect sample containers in the cooler.
The following procedures will be followed when shipping samples for laboratory analysis:
I.
Samples requiring refrigeration will be promptly chilled with ice to a temperature of four (4) °C and packed in an
insulated cooler for transport to the laboratory.
2.
Only shipping containers that meet all applicable state and federal standards for safe shipment will be used for this
project.
3.
To provide a means for detecting any potential tampering during shipment, custody seals will be affixed to cooler
and tape wrapped entirely around the cooler.

2.3.10 SYSTEM AUDITS
Performance and system audits of both field and laboratory activities will be performed on a periodic basis, as
appropriate, to assure that the sampling and analysis are performed in accordance with the procedures established in the
SLEMP. Examples of audits that may be performed are as follows:
Field inspections to confirm that the groundwater monitoring program was conducted correctly; field
measurements were made accurately; equipment was thoroughly decontaminated; samples were collected and
handled properly; and field work was accurately documented.
A comprehensive review of the analytical report submitted by the laboratory to verify all requested analyses were
performed; sample holding times were met; the data was generated using the appropriate methodology, the level of
QC effort and reporting; and the analytical results were in conformance with the prescribed acceptance criteria.
Oversight of the field and data validation activities to ensure data management proceeds in an organized and
expeditious manner.
2.3.11
CORRECTIVE MEASURES
Any person identifying an unacceptable condition will notify the project manager. The project manager will be
responsible for developing and initiating appropriate corrective action and verifying that the corrective action has been
effective. Corrective actions may include repeating measurements, re-sampling and/or re-analyzing samples, and
amending or adjusting sampling procedures. If warranted by the severity of the problem (e.g., if monitoring wells
require re-sampling or if the project schedule may be affected), the WDEQ/SHWD will be notified.
2.4

Variance condition #1 requires the installation of additional groundwater monitoring wells downgradient of the
expansion area at a spacing not to exceed 500 feet. Four additional monitoring wells will be installed prior to waste
placement in the expansion area to meet this requirement. Well locations will be provided to WDEQ prior to
installation.

OFF-SITE WATER WELL MONITORING PROGRAM
Variance Conditions # 9 and #10 specify that the City of Cheyenne implement a groundwater monitoring program for
water wells permitted or certificated for domestic or stock use located within !/2 mile of the expansion area facility
boundary, provided that the City is given access to the specified water wells by the property owner.

BASELINE MONITORING PROGRAM
In accordance with Variance Condition #10, the City will implement a baseline water quality monitoring program for
off-site water wells. Following lifetime permit approval, the City will contact the permit holders listed in Table 8 via
certified mail, taking care to check for any new wells installed since the initial tabulation. In the event landowners do
not respond to the first certified mailing, a second mailing will be sent to landowners seeking permission. Landowners
will be asked to provide written permission to allow the City to inspect water well integrity and take water quality
samples for Appendix A and C constituents for four quarterly baseline sampling events and potential future expanded

3.0
GROUNDWATER DATA QUALITY ASSURANCE/QUALITY-
CONTROL
The overall quality assurance objectives for the environmental monitoring at the Happy Jack Landfill is to provide
quantitative and qualitative measures to produce legally defensible data used to evaluate subsurface quality beneath the
landfill. The objectives ofthe quality assurance/quality control (QAIQC) program are to:
•
Verify that all procedures are documented, including any changes from the protocols established in the SLEMP.
•
Verif’ that all sampling and analytical procedures are conducted according to sound scientific principles.
•
Monitor the performance of the field sampling team and laboratory with a systematic audit program and provide
corrective actions necessary to assure quality.
•
Evaluate the quality of the analytical data through a system of quantitative and qualitative criteria.
•
Verify that all data and observations are properly recorded and archived.
Previous sections of this report have documented the procedures for field sampling, chain-of-custody, and laboratory
analysis in order to satisfy many of the aforementioned objectives. This section addresses the qualitative and
quantitative criteria which will be used to evaluate the quality of the analytical data collected during field activities and
laboratory analysis.
3.1
QUALITY CONTROL PARAMETERS
The level of quality control effort will generally be consistent with the United States Environmental Protection Agency
(USEPA) Contract Laboratory Program (CLP) National Functional Guidelines (USEPA 2014a, 20l4b, 2013, 2004, and
1999). The data quality elements that will be evaluated include:
1.
Blind Duplicate Samples: blind duplicate (field replicate) samples will be collected to evaluate precision
associated with the reproducibility of sampling techniques and the homogeneity of sample matrices. Replicate
samples will be collected for each matrix at a frequency often percent (10%) or one (1) per every ten (10) samples
collected and submitted for laboratory analysis.
If less than ten (10) samples are collected during a particular sampling event, one blind duplicate sample will be
collected. Since the replicate will be “blind” to the laboratory, it will have a coded identity on its label and on the
chain-of-custody record fonm The actual sampling location and identification will be recorded on the sampling
forms.
7Trilu1dro
M
SLEMP REV1\1 Text’201 7OBCheyenneSLEMPREV-CLEAN_RPTdocx
3-1

Revised August 23, 2017
2.
Trip Blanks: one (1) trip blank will be submitted for VOC analysis with each cooler containing aqueous sample
requiring VOC analyses. Trip blanks are supplied by the laboratory with the sampling containers at the start of
field activities and accompany the sample containers throughout the project.
3.
Equipment Blanks: equipment blanks will be prepared and submitted for laboratory analysis to determine if cross-
contamination has occurred during sampling, and to verify that equipment decontamination procedures are
effective. Equipment blanks are submitted for analysis of the same constituent list required for the associated field
samples. Equipment blanks will be preserved in the same manner as the associated samples. Equipment blanks
will be shipped with the field sample containers. Equipment blanks will be prepared at a minimum of one per
sampling event if required.
4.
Method Blanks: method blanks will be prepared and analyzed by the laboratory at a frequency of one per every
20 samples or one per sample batch. The method blank is a “clean” matrix similar sample prepared and analyzed
by the laboratory. Analysis of the method blank is used to identify laboratory derived contaminants introduced
during sample preparation, extraction, and analysis.
5.
Matrix Spike/Matrix Spike Duplicates (MS/MSD): matrix spike/matrix spike duplicates will be prepared and
analyzed by the laboratory at a frequency of five percent or one per every 20 samples submitted to the laboratory.
MS/MSDs are samples in which known spike concentrations of analytes are added to the collected sample prior to
extraction and analyses. The recoveries for spiked compounds can be used to assess how well the method for
analysis recovers target compounds within the submitted matrix. Evaluation of the MS/MSD is used to evaluate
laboratory accuracy and precision, as well as to determine any potential matrix related interferences within the
analytical results.
6.
Laboratory Control Samples (Performance Evaluation Samples): laboratory control samples (LCS) are “clean”
matrix similar samples prepared by the laboratory and spiked with a known concentration of constituents prior to
extraction and analysis. The LCS is used to evaluate laboratory accuracy and method compliance. The LCS will
be prepared and analyzed by the laboratory at a frequency of one (1) per every 20 samples analyzed or one per
sample batch.
7.
Surrogate Spiking: surrogate compounds consist of laboratory derived compounds that are introduced to each
sample submitted to and prepared by the laboratory prior to extraction and analysis. The surrogate compounds
spiked to each sample are specific to the laboratory analytical method. Quantification of the surrogate compounds
allows for determination of matrix effects and laboratory performance on individual samples.
3.2
QUALITY ASSURANCE AND QUALITY CONTROL MEASURES
Four QA/QC measures will be evaluated for samples collected as part of routine monitoring and assessment activities:
3-2
708_CheyenneSLEMP_REV-CLEAN_RPT.docx

Revised August 23, 2017
•
Precision
•
Accuracy
•
Method compliance
•
Completeness
A discussion of each quantitative and qualitative measure is outlined below.
3.2.1
PRECISION
Precision is the measure of variability of sample measurements. Measurements of data precision are necessary to
demonstrate the reproducibility of the analytical data. Field precision is determined by a comparison of field duplicate
sample results. Laboratory precision is determined by examining the laboratory duplicate results. Evaluation of both
the field and laboratory duplicates for precision is accomplished using the relative percent difference (RPD). The RPD
is defined as the difference between the primary and duplicate samples divided by the mean and expressed as a
percentage. Field duplicate RPD limits are set at 0-30% and laboratory RPD limits reference published or laboratory
control charted limits.
3.2.2
ACCURACY
Accuracy is the relationship of the reported data to the accepted or “true” value. Accuracy is a measure of sampling
and analysis bias. Biased results generally arise from personnel, instrument, or analytical method influences. The
accuracy of the methods used for the analyses of the samples will be evaluated through the analysis of calibration
standards, MS/MSD recoveries, surrogate spike recoveries, and blank results. Analytical accuracy is expressed as the
percent recovery of a spiked constituent reported in the surrogate, LCS, or MS/MSD; and is expressed as the
concentration of the primary sample (zero in the case of the LCS) subtracted from the total concentration divided by the
spiked concentration.
Accuracy = (CT_ C”) / Cs
Where: C”
concentration of primary sample
CT
total reported concentration
Cs = concentration of spike constituent
M:\StoVSWPofWyo\ProjecDocs\HappyJackLandliN\UpdateSLEMPReports\2O17O8_SLEMP_REV1\1_Text2O1 7O8_CheyenneSLEMP_REVCLEANRPTdocx
3-3

Revised August 23, 2017
The spiked concentration is specified by the laboratory quality control requirements. The compounds used and their
accuracy limits are specified in the respective methods listed in SW-846.
3.2.3
METHOD COMPLIANCE
Method compliance is a qualitative evaluation of the field and laboratory procedures used to collect routine monitoring
data against the prescribed methods outlined in the SLEMP, SW-846, and the laboratory quality assurance project plan.
Laboratory method compliance is determined by reviewing the holding times, detection limits, surrogate recoveries,
methods blanks, and laboratory control samples against method specific requirements. Method compliance for sample
collection is evaluated by reviewing sampling forms, chain-of-custody, and field notes.
3.2.4
COMPLETENESS
Completeness is a measure of the percentage of analytical data that meet all the acceptance criteria for precision,
accuracy, and method compliance. Completeness is the overall ratio of the number of samples planned versus the
number of samples with valid analyses.
The laboratory shall provide data that meet QC acceptance criteria for 90% or more of the requested determinations,
which is standard industry practice. The ability to meet or exceed a completeness objective is dependent on the nature
of samples submitted for analysis. Completeness goals can still be achieved if the data requires qualification as
estimated above or below laboratory reporting limits (i.e. data of known quality even if not flawless, can be suitable for
specific project goals). If the percent completion limits cannot be met because data is qualified as rejected or non
useable for the project objectives, the laboratory or environmental compliance contractor may be responsible for re
sampling and/or re-analysis dependent on the nature of the errors.

4.0
GROUNDWATER DATA STATISTICAL ANALYSES
Annually, statistical analysis will be completed on the analytical results collected over the year following receipt and
validation of the analytical data. Multiple statistical tests will be performed to determine if there is statistically
significant evidence of impacts to any of the aquifers/groundwater bearing zones. Groundwater monitoring results
dating back to September 1982 will be included in the statistical analyses. Statistical analyses will be completed using
selected up-gradient (background) and down-gradient (compliance) wells screened in the Semi-Confined Aquifer,
Principal Unconfined Aquifer, and Perched Water Bearing Zones.
The analytical procedures used to evaluate groundwater quality beneath the landfill meet and exceed the requirements
stipulated in SWRR Chapter 2, Section 9.b.i.C.Vl (WDEQ 2017). The statistical procedures include: determining the
distribution of the data, calculating basic summary statistics, interwell prediction interval testing, intrawell trend
analysis and compliance limit comparisons to groundwater protection standards.
Statistical analysis is not required
when all concentrations of constituents are reported below the practical quantitation limit (PQL), aka reporting limit
(RL). Statistical analysis will be completed in general accordance with EPA guidance (EPA 2009).
4.1
GENERAL STATISTICS
Prior to conducting the statistical analysis, the distribution of the data for each constituent will be determined. The
Shapiro-Wilk test for normality is the default test and is used when the sample size is less than 50. There are often less
than 50 data points available for groundwater analytical results for each of the wells included within the monitoring
well network. Once the sample size exceeds 50, the Shapiro-Francia test for normality will be used.
After the distribution of the data is determined for each constituent, the data set will be examined for possible outliers.
In accordance with the Statistical Analysis for Groundwater at Resource Conservation and Recovery Act (RCRA)
Facilities, Unified Guidance (USEPA 2009) observed outliers will not be excluded from the data set unless it can be
demonstrated that the outlier results from a data entry or laboratory error.
Summary statistics, to include the mean, median, standard deviation, and variance will then be calculated for each of
the constituents detected in groundwater collected from each monitoring well.
7O_SLEMP_REV11jext\2O17O8CheyenneSLEMP_REV-CLEAN_RPTdocx
4-1

Revised August 23, 2017
4.1.1
NON-DETECT DATA
Laboratory results reported as non-detect (ND) will be entered into the statistical analysis as a value of one-half the
PQL. Estimated values reported below the PQL but above the method detection limit (MDL) will be treated as valid
measurements for statistical purposes.
4.2
INTERWELL STATISTICS
Interwell prediction limits (or intervals) will be calculated to identify constituents with statistically significant
differences in concentrations between up-gradient and down-gradient wells. Upper prediction limits will be
constructed to contain, with (1-ct) probability (ct
0.05), the next measured concentration. The interwell procedure
calculates an upper prediction limit using the background wells and then compares the most recent concentration
reported in each compliance well to the upper prediction limit.
Table 9 provides the compliance and background monitoring wells utilized for interwell analysis in each of the
aquifers/water bearing zones. The upgradientlbackground wells were chosen in consultation with WDEQ staff based
on joint review of historical data. Background wells were chosen that represent the same aquifer/water-bearing unit as
the compliance wells in each case.
In the Perched Water Bearing Zone, wells P-5 and S-9 were chosen as background wells as they appeared to be located
upgradient of landfill activities, however, background conditions in a perched water zone may be difficult to determine
as hydraulic conditions are not well defined. Please note that some of the Perched Water Bearing Zone wells have been
dry for an extended period of time and will not always be included in the statistical analysis. This is sometimes the
case for wells EE-04, EE-07, S-9 and PIE-9. Deviations from the standard analysis will be noted in future reports.
4.3
INTRAWELL STATISTICS
Intrawell compliance tolerance interval analyses will be performed for down-gradient wells for Appendix A and B
constituents which exceed the interwell prediction limits. This approach focuses intrawell compliance statistical
analyses on those down-gradient wells and constituents with a statistically significant increase over background water
quality.
The Mann-Kendall/Sen’s Slope trend analysis will be used to determine if there is a significantly increasing trend in
constituent concentrations reported in groundwater samples collected from any of the wells included in the monitoring
network. An increasing slope is indicated by a positive Mann-Kendall statistic and an estimated Sen’s Slope greater
than zero. Time series plots and the data will be examined to ensure that a significant slope is not an artifact of changes
3Trihii1dro
4-2
708_SLEMP_REV1\1_Text\201 708_CheyenneSLEMP_REV-CLEAN_RPTdocx

Revised August 23, 2017
in detection limits over time. The trend analysis will not be used for compliance evaluations, but may provide
important information regarding changes in groundwater chemistry, both up-gradient and down-gradient.
4.4
COMPARISONS TO GROUNDWATER PROTECTION STANDARDS
A 95% upper tolerance limit will be calculated for constituents detected in groundwater samples collected from
monitoring wells that exhibit significantly higher concentrations compared to those reported in background wells
(interwell analysis). The calculated 95% upper tolerance limit will be compared to the appropriate Groundwater
Protection Standard (GWPS).
4.4.1
TOLERANCE LIMIT ANALYSIS
Tolerance limit analysis is a component of compliance limit testing. SWRR Chapter 2 Section 9.b.i.C.VI.3 (WDEQ
2017) recommends the use of tolerance limits or prediction intervals as a method to compare background wells to
compliance wells. The 95% upper tolerance limit is currently being used to compare constituent concentrations
measured in compliance wells to groundwater protection standards. When variation in the data set is large, the upper
tolerance limit is often estimated well above actual constituent concentrations, which is the case with many constituents
reported in groundwater samples collected from monitoring wells installed at the City of Cheyenne Sanitary Landfill.
In addition, if the distribution of analytical data for a constituent reported within a monitoring well is not normally
distributed, the upper tolerance limit is estimated as the highest or the second highest concentration detected for that
constituent, whichever provides the best estimate of the 90th percentile to the dataset with 95% confidence. This
approach allows some flexibility when the data do not conform to an identifiable distribution and standard
mathematical models cannot be used. If the highest or second highest reported concentration is greater than the
groundwater protection standard, then the tolerance limit will consistently predict an exceedance above the
groundwater protection standard, even if the remaining results are non-detects at the reporting limit. Historical
statistical analysis has shown that many of the constituent concentrations reported in monitoring wells at the City of
Cheyenne Landfill are not normally distributed and therefore the tolerance limit analysis could produce positive results
driven by a single historic extreme value, potentially falsely indicating an exceedance above the groundwater protection
standard.

50
LANDFILL METHANE MONITORING PLAN
A methane venting system was installed in the filled areas at the south end of the landfill before placing the final cap in
1991. Quarterly monitoring of methane concentrations in these vents was conducted from March 1992 through
September 1993. Methane concentrations over this period were reduced from greater than 1,000 parts per million
(ppm) to less than 1.0 ppm. Methane monitoring was discontinued along the south end of the landfill in September
1993.
A methane monitoring well (MMW-1) was installed between the North Trench and the newly constructed equipment
building located north of the disposal cells. Methane concentrations have been measured in Well MMW-1 since
September 1996, with concentrations sporadically exceeding 25% of the LEL. At that time, action limits for the
perimeter of the landfill were established at 25% of the LEL. In correspondence dated December 7, 1998, the
WDEQ/SHWD stated that additional measures to address the methane detections in MMW-l were not necessary.
Methane concentrations at MMW-1 had been more recently reported exceeding 100% of the LEL. This monitoring
point is not located at the landfill boundary and is not considered a compliance methane monitoring point. The City
has scheduled to plug and abandon MMW-1 in 2017 in favor of proposed methane monitoring probes along the facility
boundary.
5.1
METHANE MONITORING AT THE LANDFILL BOUNDARY
Refer to the Addendum to the Sanitary Landfill Environmental Monitoring Plan (SLEMP), Methane Gas Sampling and
Analysis Plan, Happy Jack Landfill, prepared by Golder and Associates, Inc. (2017; Appendix H-2 in Lifetime Permit),
for the monitoring procedures for proposed gas monitoring probes located along the facility boundary. Methane
monitoring at the facility boundary will be conducted quarterly in February, April, August, and December each year.
5.2
METHANE MONITORING OF EQUIPMENT BUILDINGS
The equipment building is constructed on a slab on grade beneath the shop area and on grade in the equipment storage
bays. The building has a flexible membrane liner buried beneath the earth floor and concrete slab. The building is not
equipped with a methane venting system. However, the building is equipped with two wind propelled turbine
ventilators over the garage area and exhaust fans over the garage and shop area. The shop area and recycle building
will also be monitored. Overhead doors on the buildings being monitored will be closed for a minimum of one-hour
before testing.
7O8_SLEMP_REV11
ext\201 708_CheyenneSLEMPREV-CLEAN_RPTdocx
5-1

Revised August 23, 2017
A portable gas meter capable of monitoring oxygen and methane concentrations and % LEL will be utilized. Before
collecting readings, the meter will be allowed to warm up and calibrated using methane as the calibration standard.
Ambient air concentrations shall be recorded over a five-minute interval. The meter will be placed at a height of
approximately three feet above the ground and allowed to equilibrate. Methane, oxygen, and percent LEL will be
recorded every minute over a five-minute interval. After completing monitoring in the equipment area, the technician
will proceed to the shop area and recycle building and repeat the sampling in those locations. Ambient air
concentrations will be recorded on the methane monitoring field forms or similar forms included in Attachment B.

Revised August 23, 2017
6.0
NOTIFICATION REQUIREMENTS
This section outlines the notification requirements and follow up actions for:
•
Methane concentration above 25% LEL in facility buildings or above 100% LEL at compliance points at the
landfill boundary
Groundwater detections above GWPS
•
Statistically significant increases in contaminant concentrations in groundwater
6.1
METHANE MONITORING NOTIFICATION REQUIREMENTS
If a methane level exceeding 25% of the LEL is detected in the landfill equipment building, shop building and/or
recycle building, the building should be evacuated immediately, electricity shut off; contact made with the City of
Cheyenne Public Works Director’s office, the City of Cheyenne Fire Department, and the WDEQ/SHWD. Within
seven (7) days of the exceedance, a copy of the methane test data will be placed in the operating record, along with a
written description of the steps taken to protect human health. Within 60 days of the exceedance, a remediation plan
will be implemented, which has been approved by the WDEQ/SHWD, and a copy of that plan placed in the operating
record.
Please refer to the “Addendum to the Sanitary Landfill Environmental Monitoring Plan (SLEMP), Methane Gas
Sampling and Analysis Plan, Happy Jack Landfill”, prepared by Golder and Associates, Inc. (2017; Appendix H-2 in
Lifetime Permit), for an explanation of the notification requirements should methane levels exceed the LEL at the gas
monitoring probes to be located along the facility boundary.
6.2
DETECTION MONITORING NOTIFICATION REQUIREMENTS
If the concentration of an Appendix A constituent in any downgradient well located in the Perched Water Bearing
Zone, Principal Unconfined Aquifer, or Semi-Confined Aquifer represents a statistically significant increase over
background water quality during an annual review of available data, the City of Cheyenne Public Work’s Office will be
immediately notified. The WDEQ/SHWD will be contacted and a written report placed in the facility operating record
within 14 days. Assessment Monitoring will begin within 90 days or in an acceptable time frame approved by the
Administrator. Alternatively, the City may demonstrate to the WDEQ/SHWD that the statistically significant water
quality difference is not due to the solid waste disposal facility, but that the difference is due to another source of
pollution, error in sampling, analysis, or statistical evaluation, or natural variation in groundwater quality. The City of
Cheyenne shall prepare a report documenting this demonstration, and following approval by the WDEQ/SHWD place
3’Trih9dro
708_SLEMP_REV1\l _Text2O1 708_CheyenneSlEMP_REV-CLEAN_RPTdocx
6-1

Revised August 23, 2017
the report in the operating record for the facility. If the report is approved, the City of Cheyenne shall continue
Detection Monitoring. If, after 90 days, a successful demonstration is not made, the City of Cheyenne will initiate an
Assessment Monitoring program as required in SWRR Chapter 2, Section 9 unless an alternate timeframe is approved
by the Administrator.
The WDEQ/SHWD will also be notified in writing and a notice placed within the operating record (SWRR Chapter 2,
Section 9.b.i.D) within 14 days if the concentration of an Appendix A or Appendix C constituent with an MCL or a
class of use based limit in the Water Quality Rules and Regulations (WDEQ 2015) is found to be a statistically
significant increase over background water quality. The City will request in writing that WDEQ/SHWD establish a
groundwater protection standard (WDEQ 2009) for that constituent. Within 30 days of the establishment of that
standard, the City will determine whether the concentrations represent a statistical exceedance of the groundwater
protection standard and notifij the WDEQ/SHWD in writing within 14 days should that be the case.
6.3
ASSESSMENT MONITORING NOTIFICATION REQUIREMENTS
If there is a detection of any Appendix B constituent as part of Assessment Monitoring, the City of Cheyenne Public
Works Director’s Office will be notified. The WDEQ/SHWD will be notified within 14 days and a notice will be
placed in the operating record identifying the Appendix B constituents that have been detected.
If the concentrations of all Appendix B constituents are at or below background values using the approved statistical
procedures for two (2) or more consecutive sampling events, the City of Cheyenne must notify the WDEQ/SHWD and
may return to Detection Monitoring.
If one or more Appendix B constituents are detected at statistically significant levels above the GWPS during an annual
review of available data, the City of Cheyenne Public Works Director’s Office will be notified. Within 14 days of this
finding, a written report will be placed in the operating record identifying the Appendix B constituents (SWRR
Chapter 2, Section 9.b.i.E). The WDEQ/SHWD Administrator, and appropriate local government officials as
determined by the Administrator, will be notified. The City is also required to notify all persons who own or reside on
the land that directly overlies any part of the plume of contamination, if that plume has migrated off-site.

7.0
REPORTING REQUIREMENTS
This section details the frequency and content of the routine progress reports submitted to the WDEQ/SHWD.
7.1
EVENT REPORT
A summary event report will be submitted to WDEQ/SHWD for the April and August monitoring events through the
operating life of the landfill and through the post-closure period. Paper copies of each event report will be submitted
within 60 days of receipt of complete and accurate analytical results along with electronic data, in the format specified
by the WDEQ/SHWD Administrator. The event report will include:
•
List of monitoring wells included in the Groundwater Monitoring Program
•
Appendices containing field data, laboratory analytical data, quality control data, and chain of custody forms
Fluid level summary tables and electronic data
•
Groundwater analytical data tables and electronic data
•
Potentiometric surface maps for the Semi-Confined and Principal Unconfined Aquifers
Gradient, flow direction and velocity of groundwater in the Semi-Confined and Principal Unconfined Aquifers
•
Results of quarterly methane monitoring
Results of off-site monitoring events, if applicable
7.2
ANNUAL REPORT
Paper copies of a comprehensive annual report will be submitted to the WDEQ/SHWD prior to April 1 of the following
year, along with required electronic data in the format specified by the WDEQ/SHWD Administrator. The annual
monitoring period is January 1 through December 31. The annual reports will include:
•
Summary of field and laboratory work completed during the previous year
Documentation of operating procedures and field methods utilized
•
Analytical data summary tables including methane monitoring results at both the facility boundary and the facility
structures in accordance with May 6, 2008 Memorandum (Groundwater Monitoring Data Reporting Format for
Municipal Solid Waste Landfills) and Section 3.2 of the Methane Gas Sampling and Analysis Plan (Golder and
Associates 2017; Appendix H-2 in Lifetime Permit)
Annual monitoring leachate data from NT-5
7O8_CheyenneSLEMP_REV-CLEANRPT.docx
7-1

Revised August 23, 2017
•
Description of statistical analyses employed
•
A summary of results from the statistical analyses and significant statistical increases
•
Data disk of analytical results
•
Summary of off-site monitoring data, if applicable
•
Conclusions regarding the potential impact of landfill activities upon each aquifer and water bearing zone
•
Recommendations for modifications to the Environmental Monitoring Program

Notes:
‘The regulatory requirements pertain only to the list of substances; the right hand columns (Methods and PQL) are given for
informational purposes Only. See also footnotes 5 and 6.
2Common names are those widely used in government regulations, scientific publications, and commerce; synonyms exist
for many chemicals.
3Chemical Abstracts Service registry number. Where “Total” is entered, all species in the groundwater that contain this
element are included.
4CAS index names are those used in the 9th Collective Index.
5Suggested Methods refer to analytical procedure numbers used in EPA Report SW-846 “Test Methods for Evaluating Solid
Waste”, third edition, November 1986, as revised, December 1987. Analytical details can be found in SW-846 and in
documentation on file at the Department. CAUTION: The methods listed are representative SW-846 procedures and may
not always be the most suitable method(s) for monitoring an analyte under the regulations.
6Practical Quantitation Limits (PQLs) are the lowest concentrations of analytes in groundwaters that can be reliably
determined within specified limits of precision and accuracy by the indicated methods under routine laboratory operating
conditions. The PQL5 listed are generally stated to one significant figure. PQLs are based on 5 mL samples for volatile
organics and 1 L samples for semivolatile organics. CAUTION: The PQL values in many cases are based only on a general
estimate for the method and not on a determination for individual compounds; PQL5 are not a part of the regulation.

CITY OF CHEYENNE
CONTRACT MODIFICATION
Contract Modification:
# 2
Project:
Happy Jack Landfill Environmental Monitoring
Project Number:
Contract # 6798
Contractor:
Trihydro
Date Prepared:
January 19, 2021
The above referenced contract is amended or modified as set forth in the following and these
amendments and modifications of the plans and specifications are agreed to by the parties. All
other aspects of the original agreement remain unchanged and in full force and effect.
I. Description of Changes
The changes provide for the renewal of the Contract #6798, Happy Jack Environmental
Monitoring. The Contract will be extended for an additional three (3) years, from March 1, 2021
through March 1, 2024. Trihydro agrees to perform all onsite well monitoring and testing as
detailed in the original contract.
In addition, this contract modification will extend the timeframe for Trihydro to perform offsite
well monitoring for an additional year, from March 1, 2021 to March 1, 2022.
This modification will increase the amount of the contract by $230,707.00.
Original Contract Amount:
$171,912.00

$230,707.00

$461,414.00

months:

shall

a

(electronic
is
acceptable) of a

1 of each contract year. Consultant shall
incorporate City comments and submit one electronic and one paper copy of final report to the
City and electronic deliverables to WDEQ per the requirements by April 1 of each contract year.
Methane
Monitoring:
Consultant shall perform methane monitoring in the Construction
Demolition Recycling Facility, Landfill Equipment Shelter Shop Area and the fourteen (14)
methane monitoring wells surrounding the permitted boundaiy. Methane monitoring shall be

IV. Summary
This modification increases the amount of the contract by $230,707.00 and extends the contract
for one additional three-year period. This modification includes groundwater sampling and
analysis, groundwater gauging, methane monitoring and reports at the same frequency and
number as included in original contract. The total cost of the contract will not exceed
$461,414.00 without a written change order signed by both parties.

MEMORANDUM 
TO: 
 
Mayor Patrick Collins, and City Council 
FROM: 
 
TJ Barttelbort, Purchasing Manager 
DATE: 
 
February 7, 2024 
SUBJECT: 
Consideration of Bid #S-7-24 for an Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and 
JTL Group Inc. d/b/a Knife River, for the Airport Parkway project 
 ______________________________________________ 
Bid #S-7-24 was solicited for the Airport Parkway project.  Project vicinity map included. 
 
Three (3) bidders responded to this bid, and the bid tabulation is attached.  The Bids were received 
from: 
1. JTL Group Inc. d/b/a Knife River.  Cheyenne, Wyoming. 
2. Reiman Corp.  Cheyenne, Wyoming. 
3. Simon Contractors.  Cheyenne, Wyoming. 
 
The bids were reviewed by Sam Berta (Engineering), and Tom Cobb (Engineering) and their 
recommendation is to accept the lowest responsive bid from JTL Group Inc. d/b/a Knife River. 
 
The total Bid Award for both Base Bid and Bid Alternate #1, is $3,601,036.35 
 
Project Completion is scheduled for December 2024. 
 
Funding:  
020-26-2616-42-43495 (1% Sales Tax 19-22 / Mill and Overlay Projects)

CITY OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING 
AGREEMENT 
 
Bid No. S-7-24 
 
Contract No. _______ 
 
THIS AGREEMENT, entered into this ____ day of ___________, _____, by and between 
the CITY OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, hereinafter referred to as the “CITY”, and JTL Group 
Inc. d/b/a Knife River., hereinafter referred to as the “CONTRACTOR”. 
 
WITNESSETH that the Contractor and the City, for the considerations stated herein, 
mutually agree as follows: 
 
ARTICLE 1.  STATEMENT OF WORK.  The Contractor shall furnish all supervision, 
technical personnel, labor, materials, machinery, tools, equipment, and services, including utility 
and transportation services, and perform and complete all work in an efficient and workmanlike 
manner in the construction of the “Airport Parkway” project all in strict accordance with the 
Contract Documents including all addenda thereto, numbered and dated: Addendum One (1), 
dated January 17, 2024, and Addendum Two (2), dated January 23, 2024. 
 
ARTICLE 2.  RESPONSIBLE DESIGNEE FOR THE CITY.  The Contractor shall in any 
and all matters relating to the scope of services to be provided under this Contract or any other 
provisions herein, contact the City Engineer, or his/her designated representative. 
 
ARTICLE 3.  TIME FRAME FOR COMPLETION.  The services to be performed under 
this Agreement shall commence on the date stipulated in the “Notice to Proceed” that will be issued 
by the City.  The work shall be completed by December 1, 2024.  If the work has not been 
completed within the time stipulated above, including any extensions of time issued by the City 
for excusable delays, the Contractor and his/her sureties shall pay the City fixed, agreed liquidated 
damages, as stipulated in the Supplemental Conditions, for each calendar day of delay until the 
work is completed. 
 
ARTICLE 4.  COMPENSATION AND METHOD OF PAYMENT.  The CITY will pay 
the CONTRACTOR for the performance of the Contract in current funds, for the BASE BID, the 
sum of Two Million, Six Hundred Sixty-Five Thousand, One Hundred Seventy-One Dollars, 
and Thirty Cents ($2,665,171.30), for BID ALTERNATE ONE, the sum of Nine Hundred 
Thirty-Five Thousand, Eight Hundred Sixty-Five Dollars, and Five Cents ($935,865.05), for 
a Grand Total of Three Million, Six Hundred One Thousand, Thirty-Six Dollars, and Thirty-
Five Cents ($3,601,036.35). In the event there are changes in the estimated quantities shown on 
the Bid Proposal, the unit prices multiplied by the actual quantities shall govern, and the total 
contract amount will be adjusted accordingly.  The City agrees to pay the above amount for 
contractual services in the following manner, upon receipt of appropriate documentation: 
 
a. 
The Contractor will be paid on a monthly basis for percentage of estimated 
work completed.  Submittal will be at least seven (7) business days prior to the payable due 
date as established annually by the City Treasurer’s Office.  The pay request shall be 
10:46 am, Feb 07 2024

Page 2 of 2 
submitted on the Contract Payment Request Form and Itemized Pay Request or the AIA 
Documents G702 and G703.  The engineer will review the estimate for approval prior to 
payment. 
b. 
The City will withhold five percent (5%) of the dollar value of the work 
completed for a minimum of forty-one (41) calendar days after Notice of Final Settlement 
has been published in accordance with Wyo. Stat. §16-6-116.  Upon completion of the 
work under this Contract, the Contractor shall submit a Contractor’s Certificate of 
Completion; the Consent of Surety; Final Waivers of Lien from the Contractor, and all 
Sub-Contractors, Suppliers and Materialmen; Affidavit of Release of Liens; Affidavit of 
Payment; and a current Workers Compensation Certificate.  Final payment will not be 
made until the above documents have been received by the City and all items on the Punch 
List have been completed, and the advertising requirements have been met. 
 
ARTICLE 5.  CONTRACT.  The executed Contract Documents shall consist of the 
following: 
 
1. This Agreement;  
2. Addenda; 
3. Invitation for Bids; 
4. Instructions to Bidders; 
5. Signed Bid Proposal; 
 
6. General Conditions and Insurance; 
7. Supplemental Conditions; 
8. Part IV Forms & Notices; 
9. Specifications and Special 
Provisions; and  
10. Drawings. 
 
This Agreement, together with other documents enumerated in this Article 5, which said 
other documents are as fully a part of the Contract as if hereto attached or herein repeated, forms 
the Contract between the parties hereto. 
 
IN WITNESS WHEREOF, THAT the governing body of the City of Cheyenne has 
authorized the Mayor as Executive Officer of the City to enter into this Agreement, and that the 
parties hereto have caused this Agreement to be executed on the day and year in the first part herein 
written. 
 
ATTEST: 
 
 
 
 
 
CITY OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING

CITY OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING 
AGREEMENT 
 
Bid No. S-1-24 
 
Contract No. _______ 
 
THIS AGREEMENT, entered into this ____ day of ___________, 2024, by and between 
the CITY OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, hereinafter referred to as the “CITY”, and Reiman & 
Aztec JV Limited Partnership, hereinafter referred to as the “CONTRACTOR”. 
 
WITNESSETH that the Contractor and the City, for the considerations stated herein, 
mutually agree as follows: 
 
ARTICLE 1.  STATEMENT OF WORK.  The Contractor shall furnish all supervision, 
technical personnel, labor, materials, machinery, tools, equipment, and services, including utility 
and transportation services, and perform and complete all work in an efficient and workmanlike 
manner in the construction of the “Dell Range - Van Buren Improvements” project all in strict 
accordance with the Contract Documents including all addenda thereto, numbered and dated: 
Addendum One (1), dated February 7, 2024. 
 
ARTICLE 2.  RESPONSIBLE DESIGNEE FOR THE CITY.  The Contractor shall in any 
and all matters relating to the scope of services to be provided under this Contract or any other 
provisions herein, contact the City Engineer, or his/her designated representative. 
 
ARTICLE 3.  TIME FRAME FOR COMPLETION.  The services to be performed under 
this Agreement shall commence on the date stipulated in the “Notice to Proceed” that will be issued 
by the City.  The work shall be completed by December 31, 2024.  If the work has not been 
completed within the time stipulated above, including any extensions of time issued by the City 
for excusable delays, the Contractor and his/her sureties shall pay the City fixed, agreed liquidated 
damages, as stipulated in the Supplemental Conditions, for each calendar day of delay until the 
work is completed. 
 
ARTICLE 4.  COMPENSATION AND METHOD OF PAYMENT.  The CITY will pay 
the CONTRACTOR for the performance of the Contract in current funds, for the BASE BID, the 
sum of Nine Million, Sixty-Eight Thousand, Four Hundred Twenty-Three Dollars, and Sixty-
Five Cents ($9,068,423.65), for BID ALTERNATE TWO, the sum of Forty-Two Thousand, 
Five Hundred Dollars ($42,500.00), for BID ALTERNATE THREE, the sum of One 
Hundred Seventy-Six Thousand, Four Hundred Dollars ($176,400.00), for a GRAND 
TOTAL of Nine Million, Two Hundred Eighty-Seven Thousand, Three Hundred Twenty-
Three Dollars, and Sixty-Five Cents ($9,287,323.65). In the event there are changes in the 
estimated quantities shown on the Bid Proposal, the unit prices multiplied by the actual quantities 
shall govern, and the total contract amount will be adjusted accordingly.  The City agrees to pay 
the above amount for contractual services in the following manner, upon receipt of appropriate 
documentation: 
 
10:46 am, Feb 20 2024

Page 2 of 2 
a. 
The Contractor will be paid monthly for the percentage of estimated work 
completed.  Submittal will be at least seven (7) business days prior to the payable due date 
as established annually by the City Treasurer’s Office.  The pay request shall be submitted 
on the Contract Payment Request Form and Itemized Pay Request or the AIA Documents 
G702 and G703.  The engineer will review the estimate for approval prior to payment. 
 
b. 
The City will withhold five percent (5%) of the dollar value of the work 
completed for a minimum of forty-one (41) calendar days after Notice of Final Settlement 
has been published in accordance with Wyo. Stat. §16-6-116.  Upon completion of the 
work under this Contract, the Contractor shall submit a Contractor’s Certificate of 
Completion; the Consent of Surety; Final Waivers of Lien from the Contractor, and all 
Sub-Contractors, Suppliers and Materialmen; Affidavit of Release of Liens; Affidavit of 
Payment; and a current Workers Compensation Certificate.  Final payment will not be 
made until the above documents have been received by the city and all items on the Punch 
List have been completed, and the advertising requirements have been met. 
 
ARTICLE 5.  CONTRACT.  The executed Contract Documents shall consist of the following: 
 
1. This Agreement;  
2. Addenda; 
3. Invitation for Bids; 
4. Instructions to Bidders; 
5. Signed Bid Proposal; 
 
6. General Conditions and Insurance; 
7. Supplemental Conditions; 
8. Part IV Forms & Notices; 
9. Specifications and Special 
Provisions; and  
10. Drawings. 
 
This Agreement, together with other documents enumerated in this Article 5, which said 
other documents are as fully a part of the Contract as if hereto attached or herein repeated, forms 
the Contract between the parties hereto. 
 
IN WITNESS WHEREOF, THAT the governing body of the City of Cheyenne has 
authorized the Mayor as Executive Officer of the City to enter into this Agreement, and that the 
parties hereto have caused this Agreement to be executed on the day and year in the first part herein 
written.

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2024-02-06_TotalExtraction_17125796.pdf
MEMORANDUM 
TO: 
 
Mayor Patrick Collins, and City Council 
FROM: 
 
TJ Barttelbort, Purchasing Manager 
DATE: 
 
February 21, 2024 
SUBJECT: 
Consideration of Bid #E-12-24 for Multiple Twenty (20) Passenger Buses 
 ______________________________________________ 
Bid #E-12-24 was solicited for Multiple Twenty (20) Passenger Buses for the City of Cheyenne 
Transit Program. 
Two (2) bidders responded to this bid, and the bid tabulation is attached.  The Bids were received 
from: 
1. Davey Coach Sales 
2. Model 1 Commercial Vehicles, Inc. (dba Creative Bus Sales, Inc.) 
The bid was reviewed by Bryan Bell (Fleet Maintenance), and Renae Jording (Transit) and their 
recommendation is to accept the lowest responsive bid from Model 1 Commercial Vehicles, Inc. 
(dba Creative Bus Sales, Inc.). 
The bid includes pricing for three (3) calendar years of vehicle orders (2024, 2025 & 2026).  This 
Consideration of Bid is presented for consideration of all three (3) years of pricing, at each year’s 
respective Cost/Each.  Depending on annual funding availability the quantity of vehicles ordered 
may increase or decrease.  This bid was solicited for a minimum of three (3) to a maximum of ten 
(10) vehicles. 
The Bidding Documents contain a Surcharge and Escalator – Special Provision, that allows for the 
review of surcharges related to the following items:  Original Equipment Manufacturer (OEM) 
and/or Body surcharges applied to the cost of the vehicles after the bid opening date & Model year 
escalators.  The Surcharge & Escalator Contingency, is for an amount not-to-exceed Fifteen 
Percent (15%), of the total value of  requested items on the Bid (Specifically for the Year One (1) 
– 2024 Pricing). 
 
The requested Bid award amount is for:  
$1,563,720.00 (10 Vehicles Ordered Over 3 Years) +  
$27,680.00 Additional Bid Options for Obstacle Detection System and Router (10 Vehicles 
Ordered Over 3 Years) +  
Surcharge Contingency $70,367.40 = 
Grand Total of $1,661,767.40. 
 
Funding:  
027-14-1430-40-43201 (Transit Program / Motor Vehicles)

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2024-01-02_TotalExtraction_86809670.pdf
RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
January 8, 2024  
 
 
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning 
at 6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers and via electronic conference meeting.  Present were: MAYOR 
– Patrick Collins, COUNCIL MEMBERS – Dr. Michelle Aldrich, Bryan Cook (arriving at 6:01 
p.m.), Ken Esquibel, Richard Johnson, Pete Laybourn, Dr. Mark Rinne, Scott Roybal, Tom 
Segrave and Jeff White. Also present: Kris Jones, City Clerk.  The pledge of allegiance was recited. 
 
Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to 
be routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion. There will be no 
separate discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support 
by two other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered 
in its normal sequence on the agenda). Upon request by Mr. Laybourn, and pursuant to support 
received by Dr. Aldrich and Mr. Johnson, item #24 was removed from the Consent Agenda.  Upon 
request by Dr. Aldrich, and pursuant to support received by Mr. Johnson and Mr. Segrave, item 
#29(a) was removed from the Consent Agenda.   Mr. Roybal moved to approve remaining items, 
seconded by Dr. Aldrich.  Motion carried. Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. 
 
[CA] 
Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on December 21, 2023.  Motion 
(per Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
 
Expression of appreciation was made to Richard Johnson as President of the City Council 
for 2023. 
 
 
Election of city council president for 2024. Dr. Rinne moved to nominate and elect Mr. 
Esquibel as city council president, seconded by Dr. Aldrich. Dr. Aldrich moved to cease 
nominations, seconded by Mr. White.  Motion to cease nominations carried.  Voting “yes” – all 
members of the governing body.  Motion to elect Mr. Esquibel as city council president carried.  
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. 
 
 
Election of city council vice-president for 2024. Mr. Roybal moved to nominate and elect 
Dr. Rinne as city council vice-president, seconded by Mr. Cook. Mr. Esquibel moved to cease 
nominations, seconded by Dr. Aldrich.  Motion to cease nominations carried.  Voting “yes” – all 
members of the governing body.  Motion to elect Dr. Rinne as city council vice-president carried.  
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. 
  
Mayor Collins opened a public hearing to determine whether compliance of annexation 
conditions required by Wyoming Statute § 15-1-402 exist for about 9.39 acres of land at 5419 
Ridge Road (located northeast of the intersection of Douglas St. and Ridge Rd.). Comment was 
made by the following member of the governing body: Mr. Roybal.  There being no further 
comments or input, and purpose of the hearing having been met, Mayor Collins closed the hearing.  
 

Mayor Collins opened a public hearing to determine whether compliance of annexation 
conditions required by Wyoming Statute § 15-1-402 exist for about 29.61 acres of land east of 
Converse Avenue and south of Storey Boulevard.  There being no comments or input, and purpose 
of the hearing having been met, Mayor Collins closed the hearing. 
 
 
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Amending subsection 6.7.5.b of the Unified 
Development Code (UDC), changing building façade design requirements to reduce required 
windows on urban loft multifamily residential and mixed-use residential development (Postponed 
from December 21, 2023).  Reporting for Public Services Committee, Mr. Cook advised there was 
no recommendation from Public Services Committee.  Mr. Cook moved to approve on third and 
final reading, seconded by Mr. Johnson. Dr. Rinne moved to approve by substitute dated December 
21, 2023, seconded by Mr. Johnson.  Motion to amend by substitute carried.  Voting “yes” – all 
members of the governing body with the exception of Mr. Laybourn and Mr. Segrave voting “no”.  
Main motion as amended by substitute carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body 
with the exception of Mr. Laybourn and Mr. Segrave voting “no”. Public comment was made by: 
Jason Stephen, Gateway Construction.  Comment was made by the following member of the 
governing body:  Dr. Aldrich. (#4538) 
 
 
ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Amending Title 6, Animals, of the Municipal Code of 
the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, regarding the registration, control, impoundment and general 
care of animals and birds within the city.  Mr. Cook advised there was no recommendation from 
Public Services Committee. Mr. Cook moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. 
White.  Mr. Johnson moved to amend by substitute dated January 2, 2024, seconded by Mr. 
Roybal.  Motion to amend by substitute carried.  Voting “yes” - all members of the governing 
body.  Main motion as amended by substitute carried. Voting “yes”- all members of the governing 
body with the exception of Dr. Aldrich and Mr. Laybourn voting “no”.  Public comment was made 
by: Amber Ash, Director of Strategic Initiatives, Cheyenne Animal Shelter, Janet Stephens, 
Cheyenne Kennel Club, Kaitlyn Wilson and Tessha Winsch, Medical Director, Cheyenne Animal 
Shelter.  Comments were made by the following members of the governing body: Dr. Aldrich, 
Mayor Collins, Mr. Johnson, Mr. Laybourn and Dr. Rinne.     
 
 
ORDINANCE –3rd READING – Amending Ordinance No. 4520 Defining the Terms and 
Conditions of Employment for Sworn Officers of the Cheyenne Police Department for Fiscal Year 
2023-2024. Reporting for Finance Committee, Mr. White moved to approve on third and final 
reading, seconded by Mr. Roybal.  Motion carried. Voting “yes” - all members of the governing 
body. (#4539) 
 
 
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Annexing to the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, land 
located east of Polk Avenue and north of and adjacent to East Pershing Boulevard. Mr. Cook 
moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Motion carried. Voting 
“yes” -all members of the governing body. (#4540) 
 
 
 
 
 

 
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne establishing the zoning classification of AR Agricultural Residential for land annexed 
to the City of Cheyenne located east of Polk Avenue and north of and adjacent to East Pershing 
Boulevard. Mr. Cook moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  
Motion carried. Voting “yes” - all members of the governing body. (#4541)   
 
 
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne changing the zoning classification from AR Agricultural Residential to LI Light 
Industrial for land located east of Polk Avenue and north of and adjacent to East Pershing 
Boulevard. Mr. Cook moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  
Motion carried. Voting “yes” - all members of the governing body. (#4542) 
 
 
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Amending Subsections 6.6.3.b.3, 6.7.5.c, and 6.9.3.e of 
the Unified Development Code (UDC), changing the building façade design requirements for 
multifamily residential development.  Mr. Cook moved to approve on third and final reading, 
seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Motion carried. Voting “yes” - all members of the governing body.      
(#4543)
